﻿REVISED R THEORY — WORKING MANUSCRIPT

CURRENT MANUSCRIPT ARCHITECTURE — CANONICAL WORKING SEQUENCE

This ledger records the live dependency-order architecture of the present manuscript. If an older roadmap, historical outline, research note, or prior conversation assigns a different book number or title, the live headings and this ledger govern unless the manuscript is explicitly restructured again.

Synchronization rule. New results enter the earliest book whose dependencies are already earned. Later books may import a proved result by citation or dependency note, but should not duplicate its proof or silently promote its physical interpretation.

Numbering lock. The former Book 7 was split into current Books 7 and 8. Every pre-split book address after former Book 7 therefore advances by +1. In particular, pre-split Book 12 is current Book 13. Current Book 12 is the post-split Mass, Frequency, and Harmonic Calibration branch.


BOOK 0 — SOURCE BOUNDARY AND THE ORIENTATION QUESTION

Governing source rule

Book 0 admits T0–T3 as the sole inherited Projection Craft source corpus, claim by claim and only with the status actually earned in those books. Standard background mathematics required to state or prove their formal results remains available as inherited mathematics.

Physical frameworks used inside T2 or T3 do not enter revised M3 or M4 merely because the source essays used them. They remain provenance-bearing physics imports or conditional applications and must re-enter later through the explicit physics interface if needed.

No result from T4 onward, the S essays, loose papers, spreadsheets, or prior exploratory discussion is a premise of Book 0. Such material may later supply candidate questions or tests, but authority must be re-earned through the corpus re-entry gate.

0.I — INHERITED FRAMEWORK AUDIT

Audit Pass A — Book T0


Purpose of this audit

The reconstruction begins by asking a narrower question than the historical theory asked: exactly what has already been proved before any external physical meaning is assigned? T0 is therefore inherited claim by claim, not by title, chronology, or prior use. Mathematical statements survive only with their declared domains and dependencies. Physical analogies, suggestive names, and later interpretations have no inherited authority.


Audit rule 0.I.A

A T0 statement is provisionally admissible in the revised corpus only if its ancestry terminates in inherited mathematics and explicit definitions. If a later normalization change alters one of its definitions, the theorem is not copied verbatim; it must be transported to the revised convention and re-proved there. If a claim requires an external observable, unit, empirical law, source model, probability rule, physical time parameter, or calibration, it does not belong to the inherited mathematical kernel.


M0 — Inherited mathematical background

The admissible background consists of ordinary real arithmetic and algebra; absolute value and sign; real functions and composition; limits, continuity, smoothness, and extension; ordinary differential calculus; standard real trigonometry and its derivatives; radian phase; the lifted real phase line and periodic phase circle; elementary topology; and the usual secant, cosecant, tangent, and cotangent identities on their natural domains. None of these is an R-specific axiom.


The primitive domain is

D = R \ {kπ/2 : k ∈ Z},

with open quadrants Q_k = (kπ/2,(k+1)π/2). Absolute-value simplifications are valid only after a chart has been declared.


T0 dependency spine — certified form

M0 inherited real mathematics

→ reciprocal-conjugate positivity and inversion

→ the four named primitive operators

→ primitive symmetry and reciprocal pairing

→ quadrant-local one-generator reconstruction

→ rational differential-field closure

→ bounded and projective coordinate representations

→ seam-safe harmonic carrier

→ global scalar/topological obstruction

→ reciprocal-even normalization and symmetry quotients

→ product and logarithmic coordinate representations

→ representation, information-loss, and projection-boundary theorems.


This chain is acyclic under the completed T0 audit. No step requires a physical interpretation in order to prove an earlier mathematical statement.


A. Primitive reciprocal kernel — certified

The following definitions are retained globally on D:

srx = |csc x| + cot x,

sxp = |csc x| − cot x = 1/srx,

cxp = |sec x| + tan x,

crx = |sec x| − tan x = 1/cxp.


The positivity of the four primitives, the identities srx·sxp = 1 and cxp·crx = 1, their quadrant-local smoothness, and their reflection and quarter-turn transformation laws are consequences of ordinary trigonometry. These results require no R-specific mathematical axiom and no physics import.


B. Local generator theorem — certified

On every fixed open quadrant, one positive nonconstant scalar is sufficient to generate the π-periodic primitive state. Taking z = srx, the quadrant orientation sign is recovered from its range by ε = sgn(z−1), and the primitive quartet is reconstructed rationally by

srx = z,

sxp = 1/z,

cxp = (z+ε)/(εz−1),

crx = (εz−1)/(z+ε).


Thus the multiplicity of operator names is representational rather than a count of independent continuous generators. The primitive atlas nevertheless fails to recover the half-turn branch: its static information is π-periodic. Phase modulo 2π, a lifted phase, and winding history require additional discrete or historical information.


C. Differential closure — certified

The same generator satisfies, on each open quadrant,

z′ = −(1+z²)/2.

Therefore K_Q = R(z), the rational function field generated by z, is closed under differentiation with respect to the inherited phase coordinate x. Repeated phase differentiation introduces no additional continuous generator. This is a mathematical phase derivative only; it is not a physical time-evolution law without a separately declared traversal contract.


D. UNA sign audit — conflict resolved

The source T0 convention and the intended revised convention agree:

urx = srx − crx,

uxp = cxp − sxp.

The previously inserted manuscript line uxp = sxp − cxp was a transcription error, not an alternative mathematical convention.


The sign is fixed directly by the defining expansion:

uxp = cxp − sxp

= |sec x| + tan x − 1/(|csc x| + cot x).


With urx = srx − crx, the central identity follows without any chartwise sign choice beyond the primitive domain D:

urx + uxp

= (srx − crx) + (cxp − sxp)

= [|csc x| + cot x − (|sec x| − tan x)]

  + [|sec x| + tan x − (|csc x| − cot x)]

= 2(tan x + cot x)

= 2[(sin²x + cos²x)/(sin x cos x)]

= 2/(sin x cos x)

= 4/sin(2x).


Therefore

H = 1/(urx + uxp) = sin(2x)/4

on D, with the smooth extension H = sin(2x)/4 on R.


Source-data note. The Trig2 workbook contains the historical propagation point. Sheet1 rows 33, 55, and 76 label Unaexpon as uxp = sxp − cxp, while rows 33 and 76 simultaneously expand the same expression as |sec|+tan − 1/(|csc|+cot), which equals cxp − sxp. Sheet2 row 6 and Ai row 9 use the correct symbolic order cxp − sxp. Thus the error is a contradictory label in part of Sheet1, not an ambiguity in the underlying expansion or in T0.


No normalization quarantine is required for the T0 UNA, saw, FlatWave, bounded-transfer, or carrier layers on account of the uxp sign. Their ordinary theorem audit continues under the canonical convention uxp = cxp − sxp.


E. Harmonic carrier — certified

Independently of its historical UNA extraction, the trigonometric carrier

H = sin(2x)/4,

V = cos(2x)/2

satisfies

V² + 4H² = 1/4.

It extends smoothly through every primitive seam and gives a one-dimensional closed carrier curve. The carrier is complete modulo π, while the raw primitive operators remain singular at their own seams. Smooth carrier continuation therefore does not assign finite values to primitive poles and does not supply a physical crossing law.


The carrier ellipse is homeomorphic to S¹. Consequently no single real scalar can represent the complete carrier both continuously and injectively. A constrained pair such as (H,V), or an atlas of at least two scalar charts, supplies a seam-safe global representation without adding a second intrinsic continuous degree of freedom. This topological obstruction depends on the carrier curve itself and is independent of the spreadsheet transcription error corrected above.


F. Reciprocal-even normalization — certified

The reciprocal-even kernel

E_D = 1/(srx+sxp+cxp+crx)

= 1/[2(|sec x|+|csc x|)]

= |sin(2x)|/[4(|sin x|+|cos x|)]

contains no continuous information beyond |H|. It deliberately removes carrier orientation, descends to the corresponding reflection/quarter-turn quotient, and has a unique continuous extension that vanishes with a cusp at the primitive seams.


Normalizing the four positive primitives by E_D gives a positive partition of unity and an exactly rank-one simplex curve. These facts are algebraic. They do not establish probability, statistical independence, physical mixture, conservation, or quantum amplitudes. Stronger regularization can produce a common zero seam value only by erasing directional seam identity.


G. Product and logarithmic representations — certified with dependency tags

The positive product P = cxp·srx and the coordinate y = ln P are mathematical reparameterizations. Reciprocal inversion becomes y ↦ −y, and hyperbolic functions arise exactly after the logarithmic change of variables. This does not establish physical rapidity, exponential decay, Lorentz kinematics, or a new empirical prediction. With the canonical UNA sign restored, no product formula is quarantined merely because its proof passes through the UNA/FlatWave layer; each remains subject only to the ordinary theorem-by-theorem dependency audit.


H. T0 projection boundary — certified

Book T0 contains zero physics imports, zero empirical calibrations, zero instantiated projection contracts, and zero R-specific mathematical axioms relative to M0. Its closure is mathematical rather than physical. In particular, the T0 calculus alone supplies no probability law, physical clock, spacetime metric, gauge field, quantum state space, force law, energy scale, or empirical observable.


Any later physical claim must therefore factor visibly as

external instance

→ inferred internal state

→ evaluated internal structure

→ external readout,

with units, calibration, uncertainty, validity domain, and failure conditions supplied outside the T0 mathematical core.


T0 inheritance ledger

CERTIFIED THROUGH THE T0 AUDIT: primitive definitions and absolute values; reciprocal identities and positivity; chart/domain structure; primitive symmetry actions; canonical UNA definitions urx = srx − crx and uxp = cxp − sxp; the identity urx + uxp = 4/sin(2x); half-turn information loss; one-generator Möbius reconstruction; Riccati generator law; quadrant-local differential-field closure; the distinction between local generation and global representation; harmonic carrier extraction H = 1/(urx+uxp) = sin(2x)/4; carrier ellipse and scalar topological obstruction; reciprocal-even kernel formulas; representation/information-loss theorems; and the projection-boundary/airlock distinction.


UNA SIGN DISPOSITION: no mathematical normalization conflict remains. The incorrect uxp = sxp − cxp wording is retired as a transcription error. The canonical definition is uxp = cxp − sxp. Any future occurrence of the reversed symbolic order must be treated as an error unless a separately named counter-operator is explicitly being defined.


PHYSICS IMPORT COUNT FOR T0: 0.

R-SPECIFIC MATHEMATICAL AXIOM COUNT FOR T0: 0.

UNRESOLVED NORMALIZATION CONFLICTS: 0.


First audit conclusion

M1, the T0 scalar kernel, is certified as an inherited mathematical layer, but only claim by claim and with its domain and status tags preserved. Its primitive, UNA, saw, local-generator, differential, carrier, topological, normalization, and projection-boundary results survive the completed audit. The apparent uxp conflict was not a mathematical disagreement in T0; it was a reversed symbolic label that propagated through part of the Trig2 workbook and then into the revised outline. That transcription error is now retired before later physics is allowed to depend on it.


Audit Pass B — Book T1


Purpose of the T1 audit

Book T1 does not add a second physical layer to T0. Its principal achievement is to formalize the boundary between the inherited structural calculus and any later external interpretation. It specifies how a Projection claim must be factorized, how candidate families must be declared, how observations and dimensional readouts must be recorded, and where rank or information loss forces an explicit extension. Under the revised reconstruction, these are inherited as mathematical and methodological results only. No particular external system, observable, unit, calibration, probability law, dynamics, or physical orientation is inherited.


T1 dependency spine — certified form

M1 / Book T0 structural calculus

→ abstract Projection contract and inference–evaluation–readout factorization

→ candidate-family, admissibility, equivalence, and necessity discipline

→ observation-contract and prediction–observation joining discipline

→ dimensional readout, anchoring, identifiability, gauge, validation, and falsifiability grammar

→ differential rank factorization

→ scalar rank, coframe, metric, curvature, and complex-state obstruction certificates

→ explicit extension boundary: missing intrinsic structure must be added, imported, or left unresolved rather than manufactured by readout complexity.


A. Projection architecture — certified formal methodology

A Projection contract is a typed composite in which the external instance is first mapped into an internal structural state, that state is evaluated by the inherited T0 calculus, and the resulting structural record is then mapped to an external readout. In schematic form,

ŷ = Γ ∘ E ∘ Φ.

Here Φ is the inference or state-assignment map, E is the internal structural evaluation, and Γ is the readout. T0 determines neither Φ nor Γ merely by supplying the structural calculus.


This factorization is inherited because it is a mathematical organization of maps and records. It carries an important negative theorem with it: external declarations do not retroactively become T0 theorems. A fitted or familiar numeral is not thereby derived; a complete formal contract is not thereby empirically adequate; and an internal change of coordinate that is exactly compensated by the readout adds no empirical content.


B. Candidate universes and obstruction discipline — certified

T1 distinguishes a proposed ansatz from an admissible candidate, uniqueness within a declared class from necessity among all admissible classes, and obstruction of a candidate family from impossibility in the unrestricted universe.


Accordingly, every later no-go theorem must declare:

the candidate universe;

the admissibility constraints;

the provenance of each constraint;

the operational-equivalence relation;

the existence question;

the uniqueness question;

and, if global necessity or impossibility is claimed, an exhaustiveness argument for the candidate universe.


This is especially important for the orientation problem. Failure to obtain a preferred sign or handedness from one symmetric family is not a theorem that no orientation-selecting construction exists. Likewise, finding one representative by a coordinate convention, regularizer, canonical form, or aesthetic preference does not make that representative physically necessary. A single lawful counterexample is sufficient to defeat a uniqueness claim, whereas failure to find a counterexample is not itself a proof of uniqueness.


T1 also proves an information-loss principle at the level of the factorized pipeline: information discarded by an upstream map cannot be recreated downstream by a deterministic readout. If branch, orientation, seam side, winding, or history is erased before the final readout and no independent record retains it, increasing output complexity cannot reconstruct that lost distinction.


C. Observation and dimensional interface — certified as grammar, not instantiated physics

T1 formalizes complete observation records, operational measurands, acquisition and reduction pipelines, instrument calibration, uncertainty and failure records, frozen dataset and protocol versions, dimensional readouts, bridge calibration, comparison maps, validation roles, and falsifiability domains.


The central inherited distinction is structural–dimensional nonidentity. A dimensionless T0 output does not become time, distance, energy, entropy, mass, curvature, field strength, probability, or any other physical quantity because it is renamed. Units, scale, origin, orientation, measurand, uncertainty, applicable domain, and failure semantics enter only through an explicitly declared readout contract.


Likewise, instrument calibration and Projection-bridge calibration are different maps. Numerical proximity is not empirical agreement until prediction and observation have been placed into a common commensurable representation. Calibration and validation have different evidential roles, and validation information used to revise a candidate becomes selection information for the revised version. Empirical status therefore attaches to a frozen candidate version, task, dataset role, criterion, and domain rather than permanently to a theory name.


T1 supplies these slots but does not fill them. Under the present source boundary, all concrete measurands, units, anchors, datasets, physical systems, probability models, empirical fits, and external interpretations remain outside M2.


D. Orientation at the readout boundary — certified but limited

T1.IV gives a precise mathematical place where orientation can enter a scalar readout. In the affine candidate family

y = αz + β,

write α = σκ with κ = |α| > 0 and σ = sgn(α) ∈ {−1,+1}. The sign σ records whether increasing internal structural coordinate corresponds to increasing or decreasing external coordinate within that declared readout class.


Magnitude-only anchor information does not determine this orientation in general, because α and −α produce the same absolute differences. An orientation-sensitive constraint can break that degeneracy, and two structurally distinct exact anchors determine the sign of α within the nondegenerate affine class when their ordered external difference is known.


This is not yet a physical chirality theorem. It proves only that orientation is mathematically representable at the interface and that its identifiability depends on what information the external contract preserves. Therefore T1 already rules out one overly strong obstruction claim: the framework cannot be said to be mathematically incapable of carrying an orientation sign. The remaining question is whether the inherited internal structure itself supplies the relevant orientation data, or whether a physically orientation-sensitive observable, source, boundary condition, or law is required. That question passes forward to T2 and the later physics interface.


E. Differential rank firewall — certified

For a differentiable deterministic Projection

ŷ = Γ ∘ E ∘ Φ,

the differential satisfies

dŷ = dΓ ∘ dE ∘ dΦ,

and therefore

rank(dŷ) ≤ min(rank dΦ, rank dE, rank dΓ).


This gives the active-prediction rank bound: the downstream readout cannot manufacture intrinsic differential rank that is absent from the state delivered to it. Displaying many output components does not prove the existence of equally many independent structural degrees of freedom.


A one-parameter structural state mapped into R^n traces, at ordinary regular points, at most a one-dimensional image. The ambient dimension of the display space is not derived by the curve that occupies it. This distinction will govern every later attempt to infer spacetime dimension, field multiplicity, state-space dimension, or particle bookkeeping from a scalar phase coordinate.


F. Coframe and metric obstruction — certified

If all proposed one-forms are generated from one scalar coordinate,

e^a = f^a(x) dx,

then their span has rank at most one. Any wedge of two or more such one-forms vanishes, so in particular a nondegenerate four-coframe cannot be produced from this scalar family alone. A metric assembled solely from these collinear one-forms likewise has rank at most one.


This is a genuine mathematical obstruction, but its conclusion is exact and limited: one scalar differential generator cannot by readout or relabeling manufacture a higher-rank coframe. It does not prove that higher-rank geometry is impossible. It proves that additional independent structure must be supplied if a higher-rank target is actually required.


G. Fixed-generator curvature obstruction — certified

For a connection ansatz of the form

ω = K dη

with one fixed generator K on an ordinary smooth region,

dω + ω∧ω = 0.

The first term vanishes because d²η = 0, while the second vanishes because dη∧dη = 0. Thus scalar variation along a single fixed generator does not by itself create local curvature.


This result is local and candidate-relative. It does not erase possible global holonomy, seam singularities, distributional structure, noncommuting multi-generator connections, or other enlarged constructions. It only prevents us from mistaking variation of a scalar coefficient for curvature without the algebraic and differential structure required to produce it.


H. Complex-state rank obstruction — certified

T1 also separates algebraic complex structure from an independent complex-state coordinate. A real linear operator J satisfying

J² = −I

can define a complex structure on a real vector space, but introducing J does not by itself add a new independent phase variable.


The real transfer-amplitude family

u(λ) = (√λ, √(1−λ))

occupies a one-real-dimensional subset equivalent to RP¹ inside the larger projective complex state space. A one-real-parameter regular state cannot cover CP¹, which is two-real-dimensional. If CP¹ is explicitly selected as the target state space, an additional independent real coordinate is required. The extension

ψ(λ,φ) = (√λ, e^{iφ}√(1−λ))

supplies such a two-parameter mathematical state on the ordinary interior.


This is a rank-completion example, not quantum mechanics. It introduces no Born rule, Hilbert-space physics, measurement postulate, Hamiltonian, gauge field, or empirical quantum interpretation. Those would require separate later imports or derivations.


I. Extension certificates — certified

T1 converts vague statements that “more structure is needed” into auditable extension problems. When a declared target requires greater intrinsic rank than the inherited source supplies, the lawful options are:

restrict the target to what the source can represent;

extend the source by explicitly adding independent structure;

import an ambient structure whose status is recorded;

or move outside the regular differentiable/local framework and audit that alternative separately.


Readout complexity, output multiplicity, normalization, regularization, or nomenclature is not a fifth route. Projection does not manufacture missing intrinsic rank.


J. T1 inheritance ledger

CERTIFIED AS M2 FORMAL FRAMEWORK: Projection factorization; complete contract and provenance grammar; prediction and operational equivalence; candidate-class and necessity discipline; information-loss rules; observation and joining grammar; structural–dimensional nonidentity; units/origin/orientation as readout fields; calibration-versus-validation separation; identifiability and gauge compensation; detectability and falsifiability definitions; differential rank factorization; scalar image-rank bound; coframe and metric rank obstruction; fixed-generator local-flatness result; algebraic-complex-structure versus independent-phase distinction; and extension-certificate logic.


CANDIDATE-CLASS RESULTS, NOT UNIVERSAL LAWS: scalar and vector affine readouts; particular anchor designs; Euclidean conditioning measures; regularization rules; score functions; penalties; adequacy predicates; and any other task-relative candidate family. Their internal theorems are retained conditionally on the stated ansatz, but the ansatz itself is not promoted to necessity.


NOT INHERITED AS PHYSICAL CONTENT: any concrete inference map Φ; any concrete readout Γ; any external system or measurand; any unit or dimensional scale; any numerical anchor; any dataset; any probability law; any physical clock; any spacetime dimension; any physical coframe; any gauge or gravitational field; any quantum state interpretation; any physical handedness; any empirical validation result; and any claim that a structural coordinate is an observable.


PHYSICS IMPORT COUNT FOR T1: 0.

R-SPECIFIC MATHEMATICAL AXIOM COUNT FOR T1: 0.

EXTERNAL DATA COUNT FOR T1: 0.

NUMERICAL BRIDGE CALIBRATION COUNT FOR T1: 0.


Second audit conclusion

M2 should be understood as the Projection, observation, readout, and rank firewall—not as the place where physical orientation or a full internal orientation algebra is already obtained. T1 proves that orientation signs can be represented and, under orientation-sensitive information, identified at a declared readout boundary. It simultaneously proves that scalar rank, readout multiplicity, and algebraic relabeling cannot manufacture missing independent structure.


Accordingly, no Orientation Axiom is warranted at the T1 stage. T1 alone does not prove a global no-preference theorem for physical chirality. Audit Pass C below therefore tests what T2 adds internally: whether its real-linear, complex, Clifford, reflection, tensor, and bilinear constructions provide mathematical orientation structure before any physical handedness is imported.


Audit Pass C — Book T2


Purpose of the T2 audit


Book T2 is the first source layer in which the revised theory can test internal orientation rather than only a sign carried by an external readout. Its derived real-linear lift and complex-structure operator provide genuine orientation-sensitive mathematics; its Clifford, reflection, Pin, tensor, and operator constructions test how that structure extends, with each construction retaining the status assigned to it in T2. The audit question is therefore not merely whether T2 can display handedness. It is whether the derived and explicitly extended structures, kept distinct, select one handed realization, one global seam lift, or one physical chirality as preferred.


T2 dependency spine — certified form


M2 projection/readout/rank firewall

→ real projective linearization of the inherited Riccati flow

→ derived two-component real lift with no increase of projective state rank

→ oriented exchange complex structure J² = −I

→ explicit distinction between a complex structure and an independent complex phase

→ declared Clifford generator embeddings, grading, reflection, and bilinear structure

→ candidate Pin/central-lift constructions and monodromy

→ explicit complex-ray completion only after an independent relative phase is added

→ tensor-product composition and operator-space closure as declared mathematical extensions

→ Hamiltonian expressibility, Lie closure, tomography, and control theorems conditional on their stated extension and operational premises

→ final promotion firewall separating formal reconstruction from physical selection.


A. Real lift and intrinsic-rank status — certified


The scalar Riccati dynamics inherited from T0 admits an exact homogeneous two-component real linear lift. After quotient by common nonzero scale, the state lies in RP¹ and retains one real continuous degree of freedom. The displayed two components therefore do not defeat the T1 rank theorem.


Within that lift, the exchange operator J satisfies


J² = −I.


This is an exact real complex structure. It changes the algebraic representation available on the channel plane, but it does not create an independently variable relative phase. A full CP¹ state requires an additional circular coordinate φ; when φ is independently variable, the completed state has local real rank two. That φ is a mathematical extension, not a theorem extracted from the one-generator T0 state.


B. Internal orientation is representable — certified with declared orientation data


Every real two-dimensional complex structure satisfying K² = −I belongs to one GL(2,R) conjugacy class. A displayed matrix J is nevertheless not basis-free. Once an oriented Euclidean two-plane, or equivalently an ordered oriented orthonormal channel basis, is declared, exactly one orthogonal complex structure is compatible with that orientation. Reversing the plane orientation replaces J by −J.


This is the first decisive orientation result of the inherited formal corpus. Mathematical handedness is not missing. The internal algebra can represent the two orientations and their reversal exactly. What is not supplied is a theorem privileging J over −J before the relevant orientation data are declared.


Accordingly, the strong historical worry that the formalism is mathematically incapable of carrying orientation is retired. The remaining question is selection, not representation.


C. Seam, Pin, and central-lift nonselection — certified


The local Riccati lift and the existence of J do not force a unique folded seam law. Within T2 the possible seam identifications include J, −J, reflections, and other explicitly declared boundary maps. A particular folded passage rule therefore remains additional global data unless necessity is proved within a declared candidate class.


T2.II sharpens this result. The real reflection skeleton and a complex signed seam lift can realize different central extensions while sharing the same projective quotient. In the explicit construction, the real D4 skeleton and the complex Q8 lift are indistinguishable after quotient by the central sign. Projective and quadratic readouts therefore cannot recover the missing central ± choice.


This is not an obstruction to representing a lift. It is an obstruction to selecting one from the local/projective data used by T2. No physical Pin structure, spacetime reflection law, fermion boundary condition, or preferred seam monodromy is derived.


D. Clifford and signature discipline — certified


The real matrix algebra M₂(R) supports more than one Clifford presentation. In particular, the same ungraded algebra admits both the declared Cl(1,1)-type generator plane and a positive-positive Cl(2,0)-type presentation. Signature therefore belongs to the declared vector subspace, quadratic form, and grading; it is not recoverable from the matrix algebra alone.


The Pauli completion after complex extension is likewise a representation theorem, not a physical-spin theorem. A complete operator basis does not supply a physical rotation or Lorentz group, observable units, state preparation, detector bridge, dynamics, calibration, or empirical validation. Physical spin and physical chirality remain outside the inherited mathematical result.


E. Composition and control add structure but no chirality selector — certified audit boundary


T2.III introduces tensor-product composition as an explicit mathematical extension. Its operator-space and local-tomography results are exact relative to that extension; they do not derive physical subsystem composition or a physical handedness law.


T2.IV develops Hamiltonian expressibility, Lie closure, control, observability, calibration, and falsification contracts only after the relevant state, generator, control, and detector premises are declared. Its own closure retains the distinction between mathematical availability and physical Hamiltonian selection.


Accordingly, T2.III–IV add no derived physical chirality selector. They enlarge representation and control possibilities, but any preferred handedness would still require a declared physical dynamics, boundary condition, preparation rule, or orientation-sensitive observable contract.


F. T2 inheritance disposition


CERTIFIED AS DERIVED REPRESENTATION MATHEMATICS: real projective Riccati lift; projective rank-one status; J² = −I; real matrix closure; conjugacy classification of real two-dimensional complex structures; orientation-relative uniqueness of the orthogonal complex structure; exact Clifford identities after a presentation is declared; bilinear and reflection transformation theorems; and the resulting information-loss and central-sign obstructions.


RETAINED AS EXPLICIT MATHEMATICAL EXTENSIONS OR PRESENTATION DATA, NOT DERIVED NECESSITIES: ordered oriented channel plane; specific Clifford vector embedding and grading; complex scalar extension; independent relative phase φ; C²/CP¹ completion; tensor-product composition; multipartite state spaces; selected control and detector parameter spaces; and chosen seam representatives.


NOT INHERITED AS PHYSICAL CONTENT: physical qubit or spin ontology; Born probabilities; repeated-preparation semantics; physical subsystem independence; physical time; an action scale; any microscopic Hamiltonian; laboratory controls or detectors; noise model; causal interpretation; physical Pin/Spin structure; physical chirality; empirical quantum mechanics; or any calibration or validation result.


PHYSICS IMPORT COUNT INTO REVISED M3: 0.


R-SPECIFIC MATHEMATICAL AXIOM COUNT ADDED BY THE T2 AUDIT: 0.


PHYSICAL HANDEDNESS SELECTION: absent.


Third audit conclusion


M3 is now frozen as an algebraic and representation layer with explicit internal orientation machinery. The T2 audit defeats the claim that Projection Craft needs a new mathematical axiom merely in order to represent handedness. It also defeats the opposite overclaim that the existence of J, a Clifford presentation, a Pin candidate, or a control algebra selects nature’s handedness.


The exact surviving statement is narrower: T2 represents orientation and mirror reversal, but it does not select a preferred physical chirality or a unique global central lift. No Orientation Axiom is warranted at M3.


Audit Pass D — Book T3


Purpose of the T3 audit


Book T3 tests whether the rank and orientation questions can be carried from the two-channel algebra into a genuine geometric carrier. Its historical essays mix pure geometry, declared mathematical extensions, and explicit Newtonian, electromagnetic, and gravitational imports. The revised audit keeps only the mathematical carrier and obstruction results in M4. Familiar physical equations remain outside the inherited core unless they are reintroduced later through the P0–P4 interface.


T3 dependency spine — certified form


M3 algebraic/orientation representation

→ inherited three-dimensional Euclidean affine geometry

→ declared regular tetrahedral center basis

→ positive Gram determinant and rank-three spatial carrier

→ continuous oblique coordinate equivalence

→ oriented volume and cross-product structures

→ declared oriented primal/dual complexes and incidence operators

→ boundary-of-boundary closure and discrete-form architecture

→ variable triad and rank-four coframe as mathematical geometric constructions once an independent temporal covector is supplied

→ connection, torsion, curvature, and Hodge structures as standard differential-geometric machinery

→ obstruction ledger separating coordinate equivalence, orientation choice, refinement, finite-lattice structure, and missing dynamics

→ historical physical laws stripped from M4 and deferred to the physics interface.


A. Tetrahedral carrier and spatial rank — certified with a declared geometric extension


T3.I begins from ordinary Euclidean affine three-space. The sphere construction does not prove that metric; it supplies a symmetric equal-distance construction inside it. Choosing three mutually adjacent tetrahedral edge directions gives the Gram matrix


G = ℓ² [[1,1/2,1/2],[1/2,1,1/2],[1/2,1/2,1]],


with


det G = ℓ⁶/2 > 0.


Therefore the three basis vectors are linearly independent and supply rank three. This lawfully resolves the T1 spatial-rank obstruction by adding independent geometric data. It does not extract three dimensions from the scalar phase and does not make the tetrahedral carrier physically fundamental.


The continuous tetrahedral coordinates are related to Cartesian Euclidean coordinates by an invertible linear change of basis. Their continuous predictions are therefore coordinate-equivalent whenever the metric and all fields are transformed consistently. Coordinate choice alone cannot generate empirical novelty.


B. Geometric orientation is representable but not preferred — certified


The continuous carrier possesses the full metric-preserving group O(G), including both orientation-preserving and orientation-reversing transformations. Oriented constructions such as the cross product use a choice of volume form, equivalently the sign of the Levi-Civita symbol relative to an ordered basis. Reversing that orientation reverses the corresponding pseudovector signs while preserving the underlying metric relations.


Thus T3 supplies a higher-rank version of the T2 result. Handed frames exist and can be distinguished once an orientation is declared, but the Euclidean metric and tetrahedral incidence geometry do not privilege one handed frame over its mirror.


C. Oriented complexes and incidence closure — certified


T3.IV declares an oriented simplicial refinement with oriented vertices, edges, triangular faces, and tetrahedral cells, together with a dual complex. A discrete k-form changes sign when the orientation of its supporting k-cell is reversed. The signed incidence maps satisfy


D₁D₀ = 0,

D₂D₁ = 0.


These are orientation-consistent combinatorial theorems. A global or local reorientation changes the affected sign conventions coherently; it does not select one orientation as physically true.


The sphere-center packing also fails to choose a unique tetrahedralization of every octahedral cell. Refinement is declared mathematical data. This nonuniqueness is relevant to any later discrete physical model but is not a defect in the continuous carrier.


D. Coframe, determinant, and Hodge orientation — certified mathematics with physical portions stripped


A variable spatial triad E is nondegenerate exactly when det E ≠ 0. Purely algebraically, once a fourth independent covector is declared, a four-coframe is nondegenerate exactly when its fourfold wedge is nonzero. In the specific T3.V parameterization the displayed criterion is N det E ≠ 0, but physical time, the dimensional factor c, and Lorentzian signature remain imported and are not inherited into M4. The sign of det E distinguishes spatial frame orientation; nondegeneracy itself depends only on its being nonzero.


Connection, torsion, curvature, exterior forms, and the Hodge star are standard mathematical structures once the required manifold, metric, orientation, and bundle data are declared. The Hodge star in particular requires an orientation convention as well as a metric. Changing orientation changes the relevant dualization signs; it does not cause the metric to choose an orientation.


Historical T3 then imports physical time, Lorentzian signature, Maxwell field assignments, constitutive constants, Newtonian dynamics, and the Einstein–Maxwell action. None of those imports is inherited into M4 as an R-Theory theorem. The mathematical coframe and differential-form architecture survives; its physical instantiation is deferred.


E. T3 obstruction ledger retained in M4


The following negative results remain formally important:


one scalar cannot generate a rank-four coframe by multiplying dα by four scalar functions;


continuous tetrahedral coordinates cannot generate new continuous physics merely by a change of basis;


the discrete center lattice has less rotational symmetry than the continuous Euclidean carrier;


no tetrahedral, octahedral, FCC, Voronoi, or simplicial refinement is retained as a preferred physical substrate once the Euclidean completion has been established; any such finite lattice would require a separate explicit extension;


incidence closure alone does not determine a Hodge map; after the Euclidean metric and an orientation are declared, the ordinary continuum spatial Hodge star is fixed, while no finite discrete Hodge is part of the canonical theory unless a separate lattice extension is declared;


a lattice does not determine its own update law, time evolution, boundary conditions, or source transport;


and physical time is not supplied by the spatial carrier.


These are exact boundaries on what can be claimed before a later physical law is added.


F. T3 inheritance disposition


CERTIFIED AS REVISED M4 MATHEMATICS: tetrahedral basis and Gram geometry; rank-three carrier theorem; continuous coordinate equivalence; reciprocal-basis and metric machinery; oriented volume and cross-product representation; primal/dual incidence architecture; D₁D₀ = 0 and D₂D₁ = 0; discrete-form sign discipline; rank and nondegeneracy criteria for triads and coframes; mathematical connection, torsion, curvature, holonomy, and Hodge constructions when their geometric data are declared; and the T3 obstruction/continuum ledgers.


RETAINED AS DECLARED MATHEMATICAL EXTENSIONS OR GEOMETRIC DATA: the tetrahedral center carrier itself; discrete center lattice; chosen simplicial refinement; dual complex; finite Hodge or mass matrices; variable triad; temporal covector when used purely to construct a four-dimensional coframe; edge coframes; and face holonomies.


NOT INHERITED AS R-THEORY PHYSICAL CONTENT: Newtonian time as a physical clock; inertial or gravitational mass laws; Newtonian force or Poisson law; electric charge and current; ε₀, μ₀, c, or G; Maxwell dynamics; Lorentzian signature as a fact about nature; Einstein–Hilbert or Einstein–Maxwell dynamics; electromagnetic stress-energy as an R-derived source law; any claim that the lattice is physical vacuum microstructure; or any empirical deviation.


PHYSICS IMPORT COUNT INTO REVISED M4: 0.


R-SPECIFIC MATHEMATICAL AXIOM COUNT ADDED BY THE T3 AUDIT: 0.


PREFERRED PHYSICAL HANDEDNESS SELECTION: absent.


Fourth audit conclusion


M4 is now frozen as the geometric carrier layer. T3 resolves the representational rank obstruction at the carrier level by explicitly adding independent geometric structure and supplies oriented frame, cell, volume, differential-form, and coframe machinery. It does not select one global orientation as physically preferred. The orientation question therefore survives the complete T0–T3 audit only in the sharply narrowed form of preference rather than representation.


0.II — SYMMETRY INVENTORY


The inherited formal corpus contains several distinct transformations that must not be conflated: scalar sign reversal, reciprocal exchange, phase reflection, quarter-turn and half-turn maps, projective sign loss, J ↔ −J under orientation reversal, reflection and Pin representatives, reversal of an oriented Euclidean frame, reversal of cell orientation, and changes of coframe orientation.


Some of these are coordinate or presentation changes; some alter orientation-sensitive quantities; some are invisible after projective or quadratic quotient; and some require additional global gluing data. None, by itself, is a theorem of physical parity violation.


The audit therefore adopts the following rule: an orientation-sensitive sign is structural only when the state space or geometric object retains it; it is a convention when all invariant content is unchanged by simultaneous reorientation; it is physical only after a P0 observable contract and a P1–P3 law/source/calibration record make the two alternatives operationally distinguishable.


0.III — THE ORIENTATION QUESTION


Theorem 0.III.T1 — Representation–Preference Separation


Candidate universe. Consider the mathematical structures admitted by revised M0–M4, with their declared domains, extension statuses, and the orientation-reversing transformations explicitly available in T0–T3.


Within this candidate universe:


1. orientation can be represented internally;

2. mirror-related or oppositely oriented realizations are mathematically admissible;

3. T2 supplies the pair J and −J when the underlying two-plane orientation is reversed;

4. T3 supplies oppositely oriented frames and cell complexes while preserving the metric and incidence identities under consistent sign transport;

5. projective or quadratic quotients may erase a central sign rather than select it;

6. no inherited theorem assigns one member of the orientation-reversed pair a privileged physical status.


Therefore revised M0–M4 represent orientation but do not derive a preferred physical chirality.


Proof sketch


T1 already shows that orientation is representable at a readout boundary when orientation-sensitive information is retained. T2 makes orientation internal: reversing the declared oriented channel plane sends J to −J, while the local/projective seam data do not choose a unique central lift. T3 makes orientation geometric: reversing the ordered frame or cell orientation changes the appropriate orientation-sensitive signs while leaving the defining metric, rank, and boundary-of-boundary identities lawful. The complete T2 and T3 closure ledgers contain no physical selection rule that breaks these paired realizations. Hence representation is present, but preference is not derived.


Corollary 0.III.C1 — No Orientation Axiom Is Required for Representation


No additional mathematical axiom is needed merely to define handedness, mirror reversal, oriented frames, oriented cells, or spinorial/projective lift candidates.


Corollary 0.III.C2 — Physical Chirality Remains a Physics-Interface Problem


A later parity-violating interaction, chirality-sensitive observable, oriented source, boundary condition, or preparation law may lawfully distinguish the two alternatives. Such a selection would enter through the physics interface with its provenance and empirical content visible. The formal no-preference theorem does not forbid parity violation in nature.


Corollary 0.III.C3 — The Candidate-Boundary Warning


The theorem is not a universal claim that no mathematical extension could ever select an orientation. It states only that the inherited T0–T3 framework, as audited and without hidden later premises, does not do so.


Book 0 orientation checkpoint


The original orientation problem has split into two results.


MATHEMATICAL ORIENTATION REPRESENTATION: solved.


PREFERRED PHYSICAL CHIRALITY SELECTION: not derived.


ADDED ORIENTATION AXIOM: none.


The remaining Book 0 task is to state the precise symmetry obstruction and extension ledger without promoting this candidate-bounded result into a universal impossibility claim.


0.IV — OBSTRUCTION THEOREM


Theorem 0.IV.T1 — Automorphism Obstruction to Canonical Orientation


Let U be an unoriented mathematical structure admitted by revised M0–M4, and let Or(U) = {o₊,o₋} be the two-element set of its possible orientations. Suppose there exists an automorphism r ∈ Aut(U) whose induced action exchanges the two orientations and has no fixed orientation:


r·o₊ = o₋,

r·o₋ = o₊.


A canonical orientation selector constructed from U alone would be a rule C assigning C(U) ∈ Or(U) that is natural under automorphisms. For every g ∈ Aut(U), naturality requires


C(U) = g·C(U),


because g maps U to the same underlying structure U.


Taking g = r gives


C(U) = r·C(U).


But r has no fixed point on Or(U), so r·C(U) ≠ C(U). This is a contradiction.


Hence an unoriented structure that admits an orientation-reversing automorphism cannot canonically select one of its two orientations from its orientation-symmetric data alone.


Application to T2


For the underlying unoriented Euclidean two-channel plane, a reflection exchanges the two possible orientations. After one orientation is declared, the compatible orthogonal complex structure is J; reversing that orientation sends


J ↔ −J.


The underlying real linear and projective structure remains lawful. Moreover, the candidate central lifts examined in T2 can share the same projective quotient, so quotient data cannot restore the missing preferred sign.


Application to T3


For the continuous tetrahedral metric carrier, O(G) contains orientation-reversing isometries. Reversing an ordered frame changes the orientation sign while preserving the Gram metric. In the discrete-form layer, consistent reorientation changes incidence/cochain signs but preserves the boundary-of-boundary identities. Thus the geometric carrier likewise possesses lawful mirror realizations with no internally preferred member.


Corollary 0.IV.C1 — Exact Scope of the Obstruction


The obstruction applies only when the proposed selector is required to be intrinsic to the orientation-symmetric inherited structure. It does not apply after additional orientation-breaking data are supplied.


Corollary 0.IV.C2 — Orientation-Sensitive Quantities Do Not Defeat the Theorem


FlatWave signs, pseudoscalars, cross products, oriented volumes, J, axial quantities, and oriented cochains may change sign under reversal and therefore faithfully record relative orientation. Their ability to transform oddly is evidence that orientation is represented. It is not a canonical preference for one sign.


Corollary 0.IV.C3 — No Universal Impossibility Claim


The theorem does not say that mathematics can never select chirality. It says that a symmetric structure cannot canonically break its own exact orientation-reversal symmetry without additional asymmetric structure, a restricted candidate domain, a boundary/source condition, or a physical law that is itself not invariant under the reversal.


Obstruction disposition


The earlier vague “chirality obstruction” is replaced by this narrower theorem:


REPRESENTATION OBSTRUCTION: retired.


CANONICAL-PREFERENCE OBSTRUCTION FOR ORIENTATION-SYMMETRIC M0–M4 STRUCTURES: proved.


PHYSICAL PARITY-VIOLATION QUESTION: deferred to the physics interface.


0.V — MINIMAL EXTENSION, ONLY IF NECESSARY


No new mathematical axiom is required at Book 0 closure.


For purely mathematical work that needs a definite orientation, the minimal addition is a declared orientation convention or global orientation datum


o ∈ {+1,−1},


together with an explicit statement of how orientation-sensitive objects transform when o is reversed. This datum is not a law selecting nature’s handedness. It is the ordinary choice required to work on an oriented representative of an otherwise orientation-symmetric structure.


For a later physical theory that claims a preferred chirality, a mere sign convention is insufficient. The smallest admissible physical addition must contain an orientation-breaking empirical or dynamical statement whose two mirror alternatives are operationally distinguishable. Depending on the application, that statement belongs in one or more of the following slots:


P0 — a chirality-sensitive observable or preparation/readout contract;


P1 — a parity-violating interaction, action term, constitutive law, or dynamical principle;


P2 — an oriented source, initial condition, boundary condition, or state-preparation datum;


P3 — any dimensional scale, coupling, or calibration required to compare the chirality-sensitive prediction with measurement.


The later theory must then prove which conclusions follow from that smallest added item. It may not relabel the orientation convention o as a physical law.


Minimal-extension verdict


ADDED R-SPECIFIC MATHEMATICAL AXIOM: none.


MATHEMATICAL ORIENTATION DATUM WHEN NEEDED: ordinary convention/structure, not a new law.


PHYSICAL CHIRALITY IMPORT: deferred; none selected in Book 0.

BOOK 1 — MATHEMATICAL CONSTITUTION


Purpose


Book 1 reconstructs the mathematical constitution beneath the later R-operator calculus. Its task is deliberately narrower than the historical theory’s task. It does not introduce physical time, probability, dynamics, geometry as ontology, field laws, calibration, or empirical interpretation. It also does not yet assign Projection-specific names to the reciprocal-conjugate trigonometric factors that will become the canonical operators of Book 2.


The purpose of this book is to establish the admissible phase spaces, domains, sign charts, reciprocal identities, half-angle charts, boundary grammar, representation rules, and proof-status discipline from which the named calculus may then be built without ambiguity. Every statement is to be read with its narrowest valid domain and status. Where a result is chart-local, it remains chart-local. Where a quotient loses information, that loss is recorded rather than repaired by interpretation. Where a later physical meaning would require an additional law or projection contract, that meaning is deferred.


BOOK 1 LEGEND OF SYMBOLS


Legend rule


The legend is an index of notation, not a substitute for formal definition. Every symbol appearing in a theorem must still be defined in the body before use. Symbols inherited from earlier mathematics are listed when they are materially used in this book. Symbols newly normalized for the reconstructed manuscript are marked as such. No Projection-specific R-operator names appear in this legend.


ℝ — real number line; ambient lifted phase line when used for x. Type: set / one-dimensional manifold. Domain: global. Status: inherited mathematics. Units: dimensionless phase coordinate unless a later contract says otherwise.


ℤ — integers. Type: discrete set. Domain: global. Status: inherited mathematics.


x — lifted phase coordinate. Type: real scalar variable. Domain: usually ℝ, restricted as declared. Status: inherited coordinate. Units: radians when used inside trigonometric functions. No physical-time interpretation is implied.


π — the usual circular constant. Status: inherited mathematics.


sin, cos, tan, cot, sec, csc — ordinary real trigonometric functions on their natural domains. Status: inherited mathematics.


|·| — absolute value. Type: real-valued map. Status: inherited mathematics. Absolute values on secant and cosecant are constitutive to the global reciprocal forms used later and may not be discarded without a declared sign chart.


sgn — sign function on nonzero real inputs, taking values in {−1,+1}. Status: inherited mathematics.


𝕋₂π = ℝ/(2πℤ) — full phase circle. Type: quotient space. Status: inherited mathematical phase quotient. No physical state-space interpretation is implied.


𝕋π = ℝ/(πℤ) — half-period quotient. Type: quotient space. Status: mathematical quotient used only after π-periodicity has been proved for the relevant objects. It is not declared a physical state space.


Σ = {kπ/2 : k∈ℤ} — analytic seam set for the reciprocal-trigonometric domain. Type: discrete subset of ℝ. Status: normalized notation for the reconstructed book; historically related seam notation is retired here to avoid collision.


Σ_s = {kπ : k∈ℤ} — sine-zero subset of Σ. Type: discrete subset. Status: normalized notation.


Σ_c = {π/2+kπ : k∈ℤ} — cosine-zero subset of Σ. Type: discrete subset. Status: normalized notation.


b_k = kπ/2 — kth seam location. Type: indexed real boundary point. Status: normalized notation.


D = ℝ\Σ — reciprocal-trigonometric domain on which sin x and cos x are both nonzero. Type: open disconnected subset of ℝ. Status: inherited mathematical domain, normalized notation retained.


Q_k = (kπ/2,(k+1)π/2) — kth open quadrant. Type: connected component of D. Status: inherited chart domain.


Q̄_k = [kπ/2,(k+1)π/2] — closed copy of the kth quadrant used in boundary bookkeeping. Type: compact interval. Status: boundary-chart notation; use does not identify neighboring endpoint copies.


O_j = (jπ/4,(j+1)π/4) — jth open octant. Type: geometric subdivision of the phase line. Status: inherited subdivision, stripped here of any later operator-dominance interpretation.


D̂ = ⨆_{k∈ℤ} Q̄_k — split-boundary domain formed as the disjoint union of closed quadrant copies. Type: disconnected topological space. Status: declared mathematical extension for directional boundary bookkeeping. It is not thereby a compactification of ℝ.


α(x)=sgn(sin x) — sine branch sign. Type: locally constant discrete function. Domain: D. Status: inherited sign record.


β(x)=sgn(cos x) — cosine branch sign. Type: locally constant discrete function. Domain: D. Status: inherited sign record.


ε(x)=α(x)β(x)=sgn(sin 2x) — quadrant orientation sign used in branch-explicit formulas. Type: locally constant discrete function. Domain: D. Status: inherited sign record.


τ_a(x)=x+a — phase translation by a. Type: affine automorphism of ℝ. Status: normalized notation. Important special cases later include τ_{π/2} and τ_π.


ι(x)=−x — scalar phase reflection. Type: involution of ℝ. Status: normalized notation. The symbol J is not used for this reflection because J is reserved downstream for the T2 exchange complex structure.


ι_d(x)=π/2−x — diagonal/complementary phase reflection. Type: involution of ℝ. Status: normalized notation. The symbol δ is reserved for later differences or variations.


[R] — theorem-domain tag: complete real phase line.

[D] — theorem-domain tag: reciprocal-trigonometric domain D.

[Q] — theorem-domain tag: one declared open quadrant.

[O] — theorem-domain tag: one declared open octant.

[C] — theorem-domain tag: complete carrier or other explicitly declared complete continuation object.

[B] — theorem-domain tag: one-sided boundary or asymptotic statement.

[S] — theorem-domain tag: split-boundary or explicitly charted extension.

[E] — theorem-domain tag: statement depending on an external import, contract, calibration, or other non-inherited structure.


The brackets are part of the notation. These labels classify where a statement is valid; they are not mathematical variables and must not be confused with sets or operators carrying the same letters elsewhere.


[x]₂π — equivalence class of x in 𝕋₂π. Type: quotient class. Status: inherited quotient notation normalized for this manuscript.

[x]π — equivalence class of x in 𝕋π. Type: quotient class. Status: normalized notation for the half-period quotient.

q₂π(x)=[x]₂π — canonical quotient map from ℝ to 𝕋₂π. Type: surjective quotient map. Status: standard mathematics.

qπ(x)=[x]π — canonical quotient map from ℝ to 𝕋π. Type: surjective quotient map. Status: standard mathematics.

κ([x]₂π)=[x]π — natural two-to-one folding map from 𝕋₂π to 𝕋π. Type: quotient map between phase circles. Status: new notation in Book 1 for standard quotient structure. It forgets the half-turn branch and carries no physical interpretation by itself.

F_s^+(x)=|csc x|+cot x; F_s^−(x)=|csc x|−cot x — neutral sine-channel reciprocal-conjugate factors used only in Book 1 before canonical R naming. Type: positive real functions. Domain: D. Status: temporary notation for inherited mathematics; canonical names deferred to Book 2.

F_c^+(x)=|sec x|+tan x; F_c^−(x)=|sec x|−tan x — neutral cosine-channel reciprocal-conjugate factors used only in Book 1 before canonical R naming. Type: positive real functions. Domain: D. Status: temporary notation for inherited mathematics; canonical names deferred to Book 2.

ξ_k(x)=x−kπ/2 for x∈Q_k — reduced local quadrant coordinate taking values in (0,π/2). Type: local chart coordinate. Status: new Book 1 notation derived from x and k; adds no independent degree of freedom.

m_k=(2k+1)π/4 — midpoint of Q_k and common boundary of O_{2k} and O_{2k+1}. Type: indexed regular point of D. Status: new Book 1 notation; not an analytic seam.

𝒮 — abstract mathematical state set used in representation theorems. Type: set. Status: Book 1 generic notation; carries no physical or quantum meaning by itself.

E,E' — representation maps from a declared state set into representation spaces. Type: maps. Status: generic Book 1 notation for reconstruction analysis.

Z,Z' — representation spaces or realized representation images. Type: sets, with additional topology or smooth structure only when declared. Status: generic Book 1 notation.

T — invertible change of representation between realized images. Type: bijection, homeomorphism, or diffeomorphism according to the structure being preserved. Status: generic Book 1 notation; not a time variable or physical transformation by default.

χ — compression map from one representation image to another, possibly noninjective. Type: map. Status: generic Book 1 notation for information-loss analysis.

u,ū — mathematical task map and its factored downstream form when a compression preserves that task. Type: maps into a declared output set. Status: generic Book 1 notation; not an empirical readout unless a later projection contract says so.

D_s=ℝ\Σ_s — natural domain of the sine reciprocal channel |csc x|±cot x. Type: open subset of ℝ. Status: new Book 1 domain notation; contains cosine-zero seams because the sine channel is regular there.

D_c=ℝ\Σ_c — natural domain of the cosine reciprocal channel |sec x|±tan x. Type: open subset of ℝ. Status: new Book 1 domain notation; contains sine-zero seams because the cosine channel is regular there.

b̂_k^−, b̂_k^+ — distinct directional copies of seam coordinate b_k in D̂: respectively the right-endpoint copy of Q̄_{k−1} and left-endpoint copy of Q̄_k. Type: split-boundary points. Status: new Book 1 notation; the superscripts record side, not sign.

p:D̂→ℝ — coordinate projection forgetting the split-boundary copy label. Type: surjective map; one-to-one off Σ and two-to-one over Σ. Status: new Book 1 notation.

ℝ̄_+=[0,+∞] — nonnegative extended real line used only as a codomain for boundary traces that may diverge. Status: inherited analysis notation normalized here; +∞ is not an ordinary real function value.

∼_g — seam-gluing equivalence relation on D̂ identifying b̂_k^− with b̂_k^+ for each k and no other distinct points. Type: equivalence relation. Status: new Book 1 notation for explicit gluing.

1.I — SCOPE, SOURCE BOUNDARY, CANONICAL NOTATION, COLLISION CLEANUP, AND PROOF-STATUS GRAMMAR


1.I.A — Constitutional purpose


Book 0 froze the inherited formal corpus M0–M4. Book 1 does not reopen that audit. It reorganizes the admitted mathematics into a dependency-first constitution from which the later named calculus can be written without hidden assumptions.


The guiding principle is simple: a mathematical statement may depend only on objects that have already been admitted and defined at its layer. A later interpretation may motivate a question, but it may not serve as an unstated premise of an earlier theorem. In particular, no argument in Book 1 may assume that x is physical time, that a seam is a physical event, that a quotient is a physical state space, that a sign is physical chirality, that a normalized positive family is a probability distribution, or that an invertible coordinate change creates a new physical degree of freedom.


Declaration 1.I.D1 — Source boundary


The formal source boundary for Book 1 is the frozen M0–M4 corpus established in Book 0, used only at the level and status certified there. Ordinary real analysis, topology, algebra, and trigonometry required to state or prove those results remain inherited mathematics.


No post-T3 paper, S essay, loose paper, spreadsheet result, or prior conversational conjecture is a premise of Book 1. Such material may later return only through the corpus re-entry gate.


Corollary 1.I.C1 — No backward promotion


A statement proved later in the reconstructed theory may not be backfilled into Book 1 as though it were part of the original foundation. If later work reveals a useful equivalent formulation, Book 1 may be revised only by explicitly proving that formulation from premises already admitted here.


1.I.B — Status grammar


Every major statement in the reconstructed manuscript is assigned a status that describes what kind of support it has. The status vocabulary is logical rather than rhetorical. A familiar or elegant formula does not receive a stronger status because it resembles known physics.


Definition 1.I.D2 — Inherited declaration


An inherited declaration is a datum or convention already admitted by the frozen source audit and required to state later mathematics. It is not claimed to be derivable from weaker premises merely because it is useful.


Definition 1.I.D3 — Definition


A definition introduces notation or fixes the meaning of a mathematical object. Definitions are not empirical discoveries and are not proved true. Their admissibility is judged by consistency, clarity, and dependency discipline.


Definition 1.I.D4 — Lemma


A lemma is a proved mathematical statement used primarily in the proof of a later theorem.


Definition 1.I.D5 — Theorem


A theorem is a mathematical conclusion proved from declared antecedents on a stated domain. The theorem inherits every restriction of its antecedents unless a separate extension theorem proves a larger domain.


Definition 1.I.D6 — Corollary


A corollary is a conclusion following directly from an earlier theorem or small set of earlier results.


Definition 1.I.D7 — Extension theorem


An extension theorem proves that an object or identity defined initially on a restricted domain admits a specified continuation to a larger domain or different space. An extension theorem must distinguish among continuous extension, smooth extension, analytic continuation where applicable, gluing of boundary copies, and passage to a quotient. These are not interchangeable operations.


Definition 1.I.D8 — Obstruction or negative theorem


An obstruction theorem proves that a stated target cannot be achieved within a declared candidate class under declared assumptions. It does not establish impossibility outside that class unless exhaustiveness is separately proved.


Definition 1.I.D9 — Added axiom


An added axiom is a mathematical premise not derivable from the inherited formal spine but intentionally introduced to enlarge the theory. No added R-specific mathematical axiom is introduced in Book 1.


Definition 1.I.D10 — Physics import


A physics import is an external physical law, constitutive relation, action principle, symmetry principle, physical state postulate, or other non-derived physical statement. Physics imports are forbidden in Book 1.


Definition 1.I.D11 — Projection contract


A projection contract is an explicit map or family of maps connecting formal structure to an external observable or operational quantity. Projection contracts are forbidden in Book 1.


Definition 1.I.D12 — Conjecture


A conjecture is a mathematically or physically motivated claim that has not yet been proved or empirically established at the required level.


Definition 1.I.D13 — Empirical fit


An empirical fit is a calibrated relation to data whose evidential status depends on the dataset, fitting protocol, uncertainties, free parameters, and validation procedure. No empirical fit enters Book 1.


Definition 1.I.D14 — Retired or falsified claim


A retired claim is no longer part of the canonical theory because it was contradicted, superseded, shown to depend on an invalid premise, or intentionally replaced by a stronger or cleaner formulation. Historical provenance may be preserved even after canonical authority is removed.


Theorem 1.I.T1 — Status nonpromotion


A statement does not acquire a stronger status merely by being renamed, reparameterized, repeated, normalized, or embedded into a larger notation.


Proof.


A name or coordinate change alters representation, not logical ancestry. A conjecture remains a conjecture until a proof or empirical promotion is supplied. A definition remains a definition even when it produces a useful identity. A fitted relation does not become a theorem of the formal core unless the dependence on data and calibration can be eliminated by proof. Therefore status can change only by adding the evidence or derivation required by the stronger category. ∎


1.I.C — Domain grammar


The reconstructed theory attaches every theorem to its narrowest valid domain. This rule is necessary because the same symbolic expression can have different meanings and regularity properties on the lifted line, the reciprocal-trigonometric domain, a single quadrant, a single octant, a one-sided boundary limit, a split-boundary extension, or a quotient space.


Definition 1.I.D15 — Narrowest valid domain


The narrowest valid domain of a statement is the smallest declared domain on which every term appearing in that statement is defined and every step in its proof is valid without an unproved extension.


Constitutional Rule 1.I.R1 — No silent widening


A [Q] theorem may not be cited as a [D] theorem merely because the same formula looks plausible in another quadrant. A [D] theorem may not be cited as an [R] theorem at excluded seam points. A [B] one-sided limit does not define the value of the original function at the boundary. A [S] split-boundary construction does not imply that its duplicated endpoints have been glued. An [E] externally dependent claim cannot be used as a premise of the internal formal core.


Theorem 1.I.T2 — Domain inheritance


If theorem T depends on antecedents A₁,…,A_n, then T is valid only where all A_i are simultaneously valid, unless a separate extension argument proves a larger domain.


Proof.


Every proof step must be meaningful. If one antecedent is undefined or unproved outside its stated domain, then the chain of implication terminates there. A larger displayed formula cannot repair that missing premise. Therefore the default domain of the conclusion is contained in the intersection of the antecedent domains. Any enlargement requires an additional proof. ∎


Corollary 1.I.C2 — Chart-local identities remain chart-local


Whenever an absolute value is simplified by fixing the signs of sin x or cos x, the resulting formula carries at most [Q] status until an independent proof reconstructs a branch-correct [D] formula.


1.I.D — Canonical notation and collision cleanup


The historical corpus was written as a sequence of separate essays. Symbols that were harmlessly overloaded in different local contexts become dangerous when the essays are consolidated into one dependency-first volume. Book 1 therefore normalizes several symbols before the substantive calculus begins.


Declaration 1.I.D16 — Translation notation


All scalar phase translations are written


τ_a(x)=x+a.


In particular, quarter-turn and half-turn translations are τ_{π/2} and τ_π. The symbol H is not used for a half-turn map because H is reserved downstream for the harmonic carrier.


Declaration 1.I.D17 — Reflection notation


Scalar phase reflection is


ι(x)=−x,


and complementary/diagonal reflection is


ι_d(x)=π/2−x.


The symbol J is reserved downstream for the T2 exchange complex structure satisfying J²=−I. The symbol ρ is reserved for later representation maps, and δ is reserved for differences, variations, distributions, or other contexts where that notation is conventional.


Declaration 1.I.D18 — Seam notation


The analytic seam set is denoted Σ, with sine-zero and cosine-zero subsets Σ_s and Σ_c. Historical uses of a bare B for seam notation are retired in the reconstructed text so that [B] can function unambiguously as a boundary-status tag.


Declaration 1.I.D19 — Operator-family notation


When Book 2 later needs a symbol for a family of primitive operators, it shall use a calligraphic label such as 𝒫 rather than the bare symbol O. The bare letter O is left available for octant indexing and other standard uses.


Theorem 1.I.T3 — Collision cleanup is representationally neutral


Replacing a historically overloaded symbol by a one-to-one renamed symbol changes no theorem whose proof depends only on the object denoted and not on the typography of the denotation.


Proof.


Let f be a bijection from the old admissible names in a fixed context to the new names. Substituting f(name) wherever name appears preserves the referenced object and all relations among referenced objects. Since the proof depends on those relations rather than the glyph itself, the theorem is unchanged. What changes is only the risk of ambiguity when formerly separate contexts are combined. ∎


1.I.E — The representation firewall


Book 1 must also distinguish mathematical information from the amount of notation used to display it. Later books will repeatedly use vectors, matrices, coframes, projective lifts, normalized partitions, and coordinate transforms. None of those constructions permits us to infer independent degrees of freedom merely by counting displayed components.


Definition 1.I.D20 — Display dimension


Display dimension is the number of scalar slots used by a chosen representation.


Definition 1.I.D21 — Intrinsic state dimension


Intrinsic state dimension is the local number of independent continuous parameters required to specify a state in the represented state space, after declared equivalences or quotients are taken into account.


These two notions need not agree. A one-dimensional curve may be displayed in ℝ² or ℝ³. A homogeneous two-component vector may represent a one-dimensional projective state after common scale is removed. Conversely, one scalar coordinate cannot parameterize a complete circle continuously and injectively with a single global chart.


Constitutional Rule 1.I.R2 — No component counting


The number of displayed components, named functions, matrix entries, coordinate labels, or plotted axes is not by itself a count of independent generators or intrinsic degrees of freedom.


A full treatment of the four minimalities—display, generator, axiomatic, and interpretive—is deferred to 1.VIII, where the relevant inherited theorems are assembled explicitly.


1.I.F — The no-smuggling rule


The formal reconstruction is intentionally hostile to hidden assumptions. This is not because later physical interpretations are forbidden; it is because their evidential role must remain visible.


Constitutional Rule 1.I.R3 — No semantic promotion


A dimensionless phase variable does not become time, distance, mass, energy, entropy, probability, curvature, field strength, or another physical quantity by being renamed. A sign does not become physical charge or chirality by analogy. A normalized positive tuple does not become a probability distribution without a probability model. A smooth continuation across an analytic seam does not become a physical crossing law. A quotient does not become an ontology merely because it is mathematically economical.


Constitutional Rule 1.I.R4 — No imported-law disguise


If a later construction reproduces a familiar physical equation only after that equation, its action principle, or an equivalent physical law has been imported, the result is a reconstruction or benchmark conditional on that import. It is not a derivation of the imported law from Book 1 mathematics.


Constitutional Rule 1.I.R5 — No lost-information recovery by relabeling


If a quotient, projection, normalization, or deterministic map discards a distinction and no independent record retains it, downstream notation cannot reconstruct the lost information merely by increasing the number of displayed outputs.


Theorem 1.I.T4 — Book 1 formal isolation


Any theorem proved in Book 1 from inherited mathematics, definitions, and earlier Book 1 theorems has zero dependence on physical observables, dimensional calibration, empirical data, or post-T3 corpus claims.


Proof.


By Declaration 1.I.D1, the source boundary excludes post-T3 material. By Definitions 1.I.D10 and 1.I.D11, physics imports and projection contracts are explicitly identified categories and are forbidden here. By Constitutional Rules 1.I.R1–R5, neither domain widening, semantic renaming, representation change, nor hidden imported-law use can substitute for an admitted premise. Therefore a valid Book 1 proof terminates entirely inside inherited mathematics and the explicit definitions of this book. ∎


Corollary 1.I.C3 — Book 1 is a normalization layer


Book 1 may reorganize, rename, and re-prove inherited mathematical structure, but it may not enlarge the physical content of the theory.


1.I.G — Dependency-first writing order


Every later section of Book 1 follows the same order:


1. declare the domain and inherited objects;

2. define any new notation;

3. prove local identities before global ones;

4. distinguish branch data from invariant data;

5. state boundary behavior separately from interior identities;

6. prove extensions explicitly rather than assigning values by convention;

7. distinguish quotienting from gluing;

8. distinguish representation size from intrinsic information;

9. close with a dependency and status ledger.


This order is not merely stylistic. It is part of the error-control architecture of the reconstruction. The historical corpus was productive but frequently moved among local charts, global forms, physical analogies, and later interpretations faster than a single consolidated text can safely permit. The reconstructed volume therefore makes every such transition explicit.


Book 1, Section 1.I — Closure ledger


Inherited declarations used: ordinary real arithmetic, algebra, topology, real analysis, absolute value, sign, trigonometry, radian phase, quotient notation, and the frozen Book 0 source boundary.


New notation normalized here: Σ, Σ_s, Σ_c, b_k, τ_a, ι, ι_d, bracketed domain/status tags, and reserved-symbol rules.


New R-specific mathematical axioms: 0.

Physics imports: 0.

Projection contracts: 0.

Empirical calibrations: 0.

Post-T3 premises: 0.


Proved in 1.I: status nonpromotion; domain inheritance; representational neutrality of collision cleanup; formal isolation of Book 1; and the constitutional no-smuggling rules that govern all later sections.


Certified handoff: 1.II defines the lifted phase line and the two mathematical quotient spaces before any reciprocal-trigonometric domain restrictions or operator names are introduced.


1.II — PHASE SPACES: LIFTED LINE, 2π CIRCLE, AND THE π QUOTIENT


1.II.A — The lifted phase line


Book 1 begins phase analysis on the real line rather than on a quotient. This choice is mathematical, not physical. It permits ordinary derivatives, ordered intervals, one-sided limits, explicit seam locations, and translated representatives to be discussed without prematurely identifying distinct real numbers.


Definition 1.II.D1 — Lifted phase


The lifted phase coordinate is a real variable


x ∈ ℝ,


measured in radians whenever it appears as the argument of the ordinary trigonometric functions.


The word “lifted” means only that translated representatives such as x, x+2π, and x−2π remain distinct as real numbers even when a later periodic quotient identifies them.


Clarification 1.II.CL1 — Lifted phase is not physical time


The order relation on ℝ and the availability of the derivative d/dx do not make x a physical clock. A physical traversal law would require a separately declared parameter and map into phase. Book 1 contains no such contract.


Definition 1.II.D2 — Phase translations


For every a∈ℝ, define


τ_a:ℝ→ℝ,

τ_a(x)=x+a.


Theorem 1.II.T1 — Translation group law [R]


For all a,b∈ℝ,


τ_a∘τ_b=τ_{a+b},

τ_0=id_ℝ,

τ_a^{-1}=τ_{−a}.


Proof.


For every x∈ℝ,


(τ_a∘τ_b)(x)=τ_a(x+b)=x+b+a=τ_{a+b}(x).


The identity and inverse statements follow immediately. ∎


Definition 1.II.D3 — Full-turn equivalence


Two real phases x and y are full-turn equivalent when


x−y∈2πℤ.


Equivalently, x=y+2πn for some n∈ℤ.
