New Projection-specific mathematical axioms: 0.

Physics imports: 0.

Projection contracts: 0.

Empirical calibrations: 0.

Physical boundary laws: 0.


Proved in 2.X: complete sine-zero and cosine-zero trace vectors for the primitive, UNA, Saw, FlatWave, H, and V layers; unified seam formulas; exact Saw endpoint exchange; H=FlatWave·saw_r·saw_x; the smooth/cusp/jump regularity hierarchy; componentwise split-boundary continuity of the bounded derived layer; noninvertibility of carrier gluing back to directional Saw endpoints; and the complete named boundary taxonomy.


Certified handoff: 2.XI audits the proposed complex packaging of the UNA pair, beginning with


Ψ=urx+i·uxp.


The acceptance test will be strict: complex notation will be retained only to the extent that it exposes an exact invariant, simplifies the proved symmetry action, or supplies a genuinely useful representation not already present in the real one-generator and carrier descriptions. No quantum interpretation will be inferred from the appearance of i.


2.XI — COMPLEX PACKAGING AUDIT: UNA COMPLEXIFICATION AND THE UNIT CARRIER PHASOR


2.XI.A — Acceptance criterion


Complex notation is admitted in this book only if it does at least one of the following:


• compresses an already-proved real symmetry law into a materially simpler exact statement;


• exposes an invariant or algebraic geometry not already obvious in the separate real components;


• provides a globally better representation for a declared mathematical task;


• or yields an exact reconstruction theorem that clarifies what information is retained or discarded.


The appearance of i alone is not evidence of quantum structure, electromagnetism, spin, probability amplitude, Hilbert space, or physical phase.


Two candidates will be audited separately:


1. the UNA complex package


Ψ_U=urx+i·uxp,


which packages two seam-unsafe derived coordinates;


2. the carrier complex package


Z_car=2V+i·4H,


which packages the already-proved smooth normalized carrier coordinates.


The first will be tested for whether it adds more than a compact notation for the real UNA pair. The second will be tested for whether it gives a useful exact complex form of the smooth carrier.


2.XI.B — UNA complex package


Definition 2.XI.D1 — UNA complex package [D]


Define


Ψ_U(x)=urx(x)+i·uxp(x).


Because urx and uxp are real on D,


Re Ψ_U=urx,


Im Ψ_U=uxp.


Therefore Ψ_U is information-equivalent to the ordered real pair (urx,uxp) by construction.


Theorem 2.XI.T1 — Exact UNA complex symmetry laws [D]


For every x∈D,


Ψ_U(−x)=−i·conj(Ψ_U(x)),


Ψ_U(x+π/2)=−Ψ_U(x),


Ψ_U(x+π)=Ψ_U(x),


Ψ_U(π/2−x)=i·conj(Ψ_U(x)).


Proof.


Write


Ψ_U=u+i v


with


u=urx,


v=uxp.


Section 2.IV proved


(u,v) under reflection → (−v,−u),


under quarter-turn → (−u,−v),


under half-turn → (u,v),


under complementary reflection → (v,u).


Now


−i·conj(u+i v)

=−i(u−i v)

=−v−i u,


which is exactly the reflected pair.


Also


−(u+i v)=−u−i v,


and


i·conj(u+i v)

=i(u−i v)

=v+i u.


Thus the four complex identities follow. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C1 — Complex notation compresses the real UNA symmetry table


The four two-component real transformation laws of the UNA pair reduce to multiplication by ±1 or ±i combined, where necessary, with complex conjugation.


This is a genuine notational compression, but it does not create a new symmetry. The symmetry remains the one already proved in the real pair.


2.XI.C — Hyperbola geometry of Ψ_U


Theorem 2.XI.T2 — UNA hyperbola constraint [D]


Let


u=urx,


v=uxp,


ε=FlatWave.


Then


(u−ε)(v−ε)=1.


Proof.


Section 2.V proved


u+v=εuv.


Therefore


(u−ε)(v−ε)

=uv−ε(u+v)+ε²

=uv−ε(εuv)+1

=uv−uv+1

=1. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C2 — Two rectangular hyperbola branches


On ε=+1 quadrants,


(u−1)(v−1)=1,


with u>0 and v>0.


On ε=−1 quadrants,


(u+1)(v+1)=1,


with u<0 and v<0.


Thus Ψ_U(D) does not fill the complex plane. It lies on two real one-dimensional rectangular-hyperbola branches embedded in ℂ≅ℝ².


Clarification 2.XI.CL1 — Complex display does not increase dimension


Although Ψ_U has a real and imaginary component, those components obey the exact hyperbola constraint above and arise from the same one-generator phase state. The complex plane is the display space, not the intrinsic state dimension.


2.XI.D — Modulus and signed carrier magnitude


Theorem 2.XI.T3 — UNA complex modulus theorem [D]


For every x∈D,


|Ψ_U|²

=urx²+uxp²

=1/H²−2/|H|.


Proof.


For real u,v,


|u+i v|²=u²+v²=(u+v)²−2uv.


Sections 2.V and 2.VIII give


u+v=1/H,


uv=1/|H|.


Therefore


|Ψ_U|²

=1/H²−2/|H|. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C3 — Modulus contains only |H|


Since H²=|H|²,


|Ψ_U|²

=(1−2|H|)/|H|².


Thus the modulus of Ψ_U depends only on the unsigned carrier magnitude |H|. It does not by itself distinguish the sign of H or the sign of V.


Corollary 2.XI.C4 — Modulus range and seam divergence


On D,


|H|∈(0,1/4],


so


|Ψ_U|≥2√2.


Equality occurs exactly at quadrant midpoints, where


urx=uxp=2ε.


As a seam is approached,


|H|→0,


hence


|Ψ_U|→+∞.


Therefore UNA complexification does not regularize the seam.


2.XI.E — The direction of Ψ_U already carries the one-generator state


The magnitude is not needed to reconstruct the named operator state on D.


Theorem 2.XI.T4 — Direction-ratio reconstruction of z=srx [D]


Let


u=urx,


v=uxp,


ρ=u/v>0,


ε=sgn u=sgn v.


Then


z=srx


is uniquely determined by


z=[ε(1+ρ)+√(ρ²+6ρ+1)]/2.


Proof.


Section 2.IV proved


u/v=z/w.


Section 2.VII gives


w=(z+ε)/(εz−1).


Therefore


ρ

=z/w

=z(εz−1)/(z+ε).


Multiply through:


ρ(z+ε)=εz²−z.


Rearrange:


εz²−(1+ρ)z−ερ=0.


Multiplying by ε and using ε²=1 gives


z²−ε(1+ρ)z−ρ=0.


The quadratic formula gives


z=

[ε(1+ρ)±√((1+ρ)²+4ρ)]/2

=

[ε(1+ρ)±√(ρ²+6ρ+1)]/2.


Because ρ>0,


√(ρ²+6ρ+1)>1+ρ.


Exactly one of the two roots is positive on either ε branch, namely the root with the plus sign before the square root. Thus the displayed positive root is unique. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C5 — Normalized UNA direction is sufficient on D


The complex direction


Ψ_U/|Ψ_U|


encodes both:


• ε, through whether Ψ_U lies in quadrant I or quadrant III of the complex plane;


• ρ=urx/uxp, through its slope.


By Theorem 2.XI.T4 these determine z=srx, and Section 2.VII then reconstructs every named primitive, UNA, Saw, and FlatWave quantity.


Hence the modulus of Ψ_U is informationally redundant for the static operator-reconstruction task on D.


Negative Theorem 2.XI.N1 — UNA complexification is not a new generator


Ψ_U does not add a continuous degree of freedom or a new independent invariant to the operator calculus.


Its real and imaginary parts are the already-defined UNA pair, and even its normalized direction is sufficient to reconstruct the same one-generator state on D.


2.XI.F — Carrier information recoverable from Ψ_U


Theorem 2.XI.T5 — Carrier reconstruction from the UNA complex package [D]


Let


u=Re Ψ_U,


v=Im Ψ_U.


Then


H=1/(u+v).


Moreover,


sgn V=sgn(u−v)


whenever V≠0, and V=0 exactly when u=v.


Therefore


V=

sgn(u−v)√(1/4−4H²)


when u≠v,


and


V=0


when u=v.


Proof.


The first identity is the reciprocal UNA-sum definition of H.


From the exact magnitude forms in 2.IV,


u−v

=2(|cos x|−|sin x|)/(|sin x||cos x|).


The denominator is positive. Also


2V=cos 2x

=|cos x|²−|sin x|²

=(|cos x|−|sin x|)(|cos x|+|sin x|).


The second factor is positive, so


sgn(u−v)=sgn V.


Finally the carrier ellipse gives


V²=1/4−4H².


The sign relation selects the correct square-root branch, with V=0 at u=v. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C6 — Ψ_U and (H,V) are information-equivalent on D


On D, Ψ_U reconstructs the carrier pair (H,V). Conversely, the carrier pair reconstructs the phase modulo π and therefore reconstructs Ψ_U through the already-proved named operator formulas.


Their difference is therefore not interior information content. Their difference is analytic behavior at the seam:


Ψ_U diverges,


while (H,V) remains finite and smooth.


2.XI.G — UNA complex audit verdict


Theorem 2.XI.T6 — Status of Ψ_U


The UNA complex package is mathematically legitimate and mildly useful for compact symmetry notation and for displaying the two hyperbola branches of the UNA state.


It is not superior to the real one-generator or harmonic-carrier representations because:


• it contains no new continuous information;


• its modulus is redundant for reconstruction;


• it diverges at every primitive seam;


• and it does not simplify the underlying calculus enough to justify promotion to a foundational coordinate.


Therefore Ψ_U is retained as an optional derived representation, not as a new primitive, axiom, or preferred state variable.


2.XI.H — Smooth carrier complexification


The harmonic carrier has a much stronger complex packaging because its two normalized components already form the ordinary unit circle.


Definition 2.XI.D2 — Unit carrier phasor [R]


Define


Z_car(x)=2V(x)+i·4H(x).


Equivalently, using W=2V and U=4H,


Z_car=W+iU.


Theorem 2.XI.T7 — Euler form of the carrier [R]


For every real x,


Z_car(x)=cos 2x+i sin 2x=e^{2ix}.


Proof.


Section 2.VIII gives


2V=cos 2x,


4H=sin 2x.


Substitution yields


Z_car=cos 2x+i sin 2x.


Euler’s identity


e^{iθ}=cos θ+i sin θ


with θ=2x gives


Z_car=e^{2ix}. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C7 — Unit modulus [R]


For every real x,


|Z_car|=1.


Proof.


|Z_car|²

=cos²2x+sin²2x

=1. ∎


Theorem 2.XI.T8 — Complex linear harmonic equation [R]


For every real x,


Z_car'=2i Z_car.


Consequently,


Z_car''+4Z_car=0.


Proof.


Differentiate


Z_car=e^{2ix}.


Then


Z_car'=2i e^{2ix}=2i Z_car.


Differentiating once more gives


Z_car''=(2i)²Z_car=−4Z_car. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C8 — Exact complex linearization of the real carrier system


The two real first-order equations


H'=V,


V'=−4H


are equivalent, after the normalization Z_car=2V+i4H, to the single complex linear equation


Z_car'=2iZ_car.


This is a genuine algebraic compression of the smooth carrier dynamics in the coordinate x.


Clarification 2.XI.CL2 — Linear phase equation is not physical Schrödinger evolution


The occurrence of


i


and a first-order complex equation does not make Z_car a quantum state and does not make x physical time.


No Hilbert space, Hamiltonian, Born rule, norm interpreted as probability, or time-evolution postulate has been introduced.


2.XI.I — Carrier phasor symmetry


Theorem 2.XI.T9 — Exact symmetry laws of Z_car [R]


For every real x,


Z_car(−x)=conj(Z_car(x)),


Z_car(x+π/2)=−Z_car(x),


Z_car(x+π)=Z_car(x),


Z_car(π/2−x)=−conj(Z_car(x)).


Proof.


Use


Z_car=e^{2ix}.


Then


Z_car(−x)=e^{-2ix}=conj(e^{2ix}),


Z_car(x+π/2)=e^{2ix+iπ}=−e^{2ix},


Z_car(x+π)=e^{2ix+i2π}=e^{2ix},


and


Z_car(π/2−x)=e^{iπ-2ix}=−e^{-2ix}=−conj(Z_car(x)). ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C9 — FlatWave and seam class from Z_car


On D,


FlatWave=sgn(Im Z_car).


At a seam,


Im Z_car=0,


while


Re Z_car=+1


at sine-zero seams and


Re Z_car=−1


at cosine-zero seams.


Thus the same unit complex carrier contains both the binary interior sign character and the smooth seam classifier.


2.XI.J — Exact fibers and topology


Theorem 2.XI.T10 — Carrier phasor fiber theorem [R]


For real x,y,


Z_car(x)=Z_car(y)


if and only if


x−y∈πℤ.


Proof.


Z_car(x)=Z_car(y)


is equivalent to


e^{2ix}=e^{2iy},


which holds exactly when


2(x−y)∈2πℤ,


or


x−y∈πℤ. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C10 — Z_car is the complex-coordinate form of the carrier ellipse


The map


(H,V)↦2V+i4H


is a real-linear bijection from the carrier ellipse C_car to the complex unit circle.


Therefore Z_car has exactly the same static information and the same π-fibers as the real pair (H,V).


It is not a reduction from two real embedding coordinates to one real scalar. One complex number is itself two real coordinates subject here to one modulus constraint.


2.XI.K — Seam behavior of the two complex packages


Theorem 2.XI.T11 — Complex seam contrast [B,R]


As any primitive seam is approached,


|Ψ_U|→+∞,


while


Z_car


remains on the unit circle and reaches exactly one of the finite values


+1


or


−1.


Specifically,


Z_car=+1


at sine-zero seams and


Z_car=−1


at cosine-zero seams.


Proof.


The divergence of |Ψ_U| follows from Theorem 2.XI.T3 and |H|→0.


For Z_car, H=0 at every seam and V=±1/2. Hence


Z_car=2V=±1,


with the sign determined by seam class as proved in 2.VIII. ∎


Corollary 2.XI.C11 — Complex notation does not itself regularize a representation


Ψ_U and Z_car are both complex-valued, yet one diverges at the seam and the other is smooth there.


Regularity comes from the underlying real construction being packaged, not from the use of complex numbers.


2.XI.L — COMPLEMENTARY-SWAP CLOSURE AND THE DOUBLE-ANGLE PHASOR


The preceding complex packaging can be sharpened. No complex degree of freedom is assumed in what follows. The starting data are the already-certified real primitive quartet and its complementary reflection. The result is that an involutive real swap, after even/odd decomposition and normalization, reproduces the already-certified double-angle carrier and its continuous complex rotation.


Definition 2.XI.L1 — Complementary swap


Let


C(x)=π/2−x


and define the real-linear, complex-antilinear map


T(z)=i overline(z).


For z=a+ib with a,b real,


T(z)=b+ia,


and therefore


T²(z)=z.


Thus T is an involution. The primitive complementary identities already proved in Book 2 give


srx(Cx)=cxp(x),     cxp(Cx)=srx(x),

sxp(Cx)=crx(x),     crx(Cx)=sxp(x).


Define


P=srx+i cxp,

R=crx+i sxp.


Then


P(Cx)=T(P(x)),

R(Cx)=T(R(x)).


Because crx=1/cxp and sxp=1/srx,


R=P/(srx·cxp).


Hence the UNA complex package


Ψ_U=urx+i uxp=P−R


inherits the same closure:


Ψ_U(Cx)=T(Ψ_U(x))=uxp+i urx.


Accordingly Ψ_U and uxp+i urx form an exact two-state complementary-swap orbit. This statement concerns symmetry in the parameter x; it is not yet a temporal propagation law.


Lemma 2.XI.L2 — Primitive norm-pair invariant


For arbitrary real a,b,


(a+ib)(b+ia)=i(a²+b²).


Therefore


Ψ_U T(Ψ_U)=i|Ψ_U|²,

P T(P)=i|P|²,

R T(R)=i|R|².


Whenever the factors are nonzero, normalization gives


(Ψ_U/|Ψ_U|)(TΨ_U/|TΨ_U|)=i,


and likewise for the primitive constituent pairs. Thus complementary partners have equal modulus and a fixed normalized product i.


Definition 2.XI.L3 — Aligned reciprocal-difference pair


The double-angle structure is exposed most directly by pairing each primitive with its own reciprocal channel:


A=srx−sxp,

B=cxp−crx.


The absolute-valued envelopes cancel exactly inside these differences, so on the primitive domain D,


A=2 cot x,

B=2 tan x.


Consequently


AB=4.


Define


Ω=A+iB.


Complementary reflection exchanges A and B, hence


Ω(Cx)=B+iA=T(Ω(x)).


Thus the aligned primitive-difference package has the same complementary-swap closure as Ψ_U.


Definition 2.XI.L4 — Swap-even and swap-odd channels


Set


Σ=A+B,

Δ=A−B.


Then


Ω+TΩ=(1+i)Σ,

Ω−TΩ=(1−i)Δ.


Therefore Σ is the complementary-swap-even channel and Δ is the complementary-swap-odd channel.


Expanding in the primitive quartet,


Σ=(srx−sxp)+(cxp−crx)

  =(srx−crx)+(cxp−sxp)

  =urx+uxp,


while


Δ=srx−sxp−cxp+crx.


Thus the aligned primitive package and the UNA package share the same swap-even channel:


Ψ_U+TΨ_U=(1+i)Σ

          =Ω+TΩ.


Their distinction lies in the odd channel. The aligned reciprocal pairing supplies Δ, which will become the cosine readout.


Theorem 2.XI.L5 — Double-angle hyperbola


On D,


Σ²−Δ²=16.


Proof.


Σ²−Δ²

=(A+B)²−(A−B)²

=4AB

=16. ∎


Equivalently,


(Σ/4)²−(Δ/4)²=1.


Theorem 2.XI.L6 — Double-angle readout


The swap channels recover the complete double-angle pair:


sin 2x=4/Σ,

cos 2x=Δ/Σ.


Proof.


Using A=2 cot x and B=2 tan x,


Σ=2(cot x+tan x)=4/sin 2x,


and


Δ=2(cot x−tan x)=4 cot 2x.


Therefore 4/Σ=sin 2x and Δ/Σ=cos 2x. ∎


The same normalization also exposes the squared single-angle weights:


A/Σ=cos²x,

B/Σ=sin²x.


Hence the swap-even normalization removes the half-turn sign ambiguity carried by the unsquared single-angle functions, while


(A−B)/Σ=cos²x−sin²x=cos 2x.


Theorem 2.XI.L7 — Canonical double-angle phasor


Define


Z₂=(Δ+4i)/Σ.


Then


Z₂=cos 2x+i sin 2x=e^(2ix),


and


|Z₂|=1.


Proof.


By Theorem 2.XI.L6,


Z₂=Δ/Σ+i·4/Σ

   =cos 2x+i sin 2x

   =e^(2ix).


Alternatively, unit modulus follows directly from the hyperbolic invariant:


|Z₂|²=(Δ²+16)/Σ²=1. ∎


The constant imaginary numerator 4 is therefore not an independently chosen scale inside this representation. It is the fixed norm inherited from the reciprocal-product identity AB=4 and the hyperbola Σ²−Δ²=16.


Corollary 2.XI.L7a — Identification with the certified harmonic carrier


The previously defined bounded carrier coordinates satisfy


H=1/Σ=sin 2x/4,

V=Δ/(2Σ)=cos 2x/2.


Therefore


Z₂=2V+i·4H=Z_car.


Thus the unit carrier already certified earlier in Book 2 is exactly the normalized circle image of the complementary-swap hyperbolic pair (Σ,Δ).


Theorem 2.XI.L8 — Differential closure of the normalized carrier


The unbounded swap channels satisfy


Σ′=−(1/2)ΣΔ,

Δ′=−(1/2)Σ².


Proof.


These identities follow by differentiating


Σ=2(cot x+tan x),

Δ=2(cot x−tan x)


and reducing with the same reciprocal identities used above. ∎


The hyperbolic invariant is preserved:


d/dx(Σ²−Δ²)=0.


Passing to the bounded variables gives


H′=V,

V′=−4H.


Consequently


Z₂′=2i Z₂.


This establishes a precise hierarchy:


primitive complementary swap

→ fixed reciprocal product AB=4

→ hyperbola Σ²−Δ²=16

→ normalized double-angle circle

→ Z₂=e^(2ix)

→ Z₂′=2iZ₂.


Proposition 2.XI.L9 — Symmetry closure is not itself differential propagation


The unnormalized UNA package Ψ_U has exact complementary-swap closure,


Ψ_U(Cx)=TΨ_U(x),


but there is no global real scalar rate f(x) on an open primitive chart for which


Ψ_U′=f(x)TΨ_U.


Indeed, in the certified transfer coordinate λ with


urx=ε/λ,

uxp=ε/(1−λ),

0<λ<1,


the two real components would require simultaneously


f=−λ′(1−λ)/λ²


and


f=λ′λ/(1−λ)².


Equality would imply


−(1−λ)³=λ³,


which is impossible for 0<λ<1.


Therefore two distinct structures must be kept separate:


1. T²=1: involutive complementary exchange of the unnormalized real channels;

2. Z₂′=2iZ₂: continuous rotation of the normalized double-angle carrier.


Status 2.XI.L10 — Interpretation boundary


The results of this section are exact consequences of the certified primitive definitions, reciprocal identities, complementary symmetry, and ordinary complex notation. They prove:


• componentwise complementary-swap closure of the primitive complex pairs;

• inherited complementary-swap closure of Ψ_U and Ω;

• the invariant product AB=4;

• the hyperbola Σ²−Δ²=16;

• the double-angle readout sin 2x=4/Σ and cos 2x=Δ/Σ;

• the normalized phasor Z₂=(Δ+4i)/Σ=e^(2ix);

• identification Z₂=Z_car;

• and the differential carrier law Z₂′=2iZ₂.


They do not establish physical time evolution, spacetime wave propagation, quantum amplitudes, local U(1) gauge redundancy, electromagnetic dynamics, or an independent imaginary physical sector. The complex carrier is canonically available as a representation of the normalized real pair (Σ,Δ); any stronger physical interpretation remains a later extension subject to the Book 1 empirical firewall.


2.XI.M — Final complex audit verdict


Theorem 2.XI.T12 — Retention decision


The two candidate complex packages receive different statuses.


UNA package Ψ_U:


retained as an optional derived diagnostic representation because it compresses symmetry notation and exposes the exact two-hyperbola geometry of the UNA pair, but it introduces no new information and remains seam-singular.


Carrier package Z_car:


retained as the canonical optional complex representation of the smooth harmonic carrier because it gives the exact unit phasor


Z_car=e^{2ix},


compresses the real harmonic system to


Z_car'=2iZ_car,


encodes the complete carrier state modulo π, and remains smooth across every seam.


Neither package is promoted to physical status.


Negative Theorem 2.XI.N2 — Complex structure does not imply quantum structure


The existence of a useful complex representation is insufficient to infer:


• a quantum amplitude;


• a Hilbert-space state;


• a Born probability;


• spinor structure;


• electromagnetic field structure;


• unitary time evolution;


• or a physically meaningful phase convention.


Those require additional structures not present in Book 2.


Book 2, Section 2.XI — Closure ledger


Inherited mathematics used: UNA definitions and symmetries from 2.IV; double-angle sum/product from 2.V; FlatWave from 2.VI; one-generator reconstruction from 2.VII; harmonic carrier from 2.VIII; and boundary behavior from 2.X.


Wolfram audit: the complex symmetry identities for Ψ_U, |Z_car|=1, Z_car'=2iZ_car, and all four Z_car symmetry laws were independently simplified to zero or one under the declared real assumptions before insertion. The hyperbola and reconstruction identities were derived algebraically from already-proved real theorems.


New Book 2 symbols activated: i, Ψ_U, Z_car, complex conjugation conj(·), Re, Im, and |·| for complex modulus when applied to complex quantities.


New Projection-specific mathematical axioms: 0.

Physics imports: 0.

Projection contracts: 0.

Empirical calibrations: 0.

Quantum postulates: 0.


Proved in 2.XI: the complete complex symmetry law of Ψ_U; its rectangular-hyperbola image; modulus formula; redundancy of its modulus for static reconstruction; recovery of z from its complex direction; recovery of the harmonic carrier from Ψ_U; the exact unit-phasor form Z_car=e^{2ix}; complex linearization Z_car'=2iZ_car; complete phasor symmetry laws; exact π-fibers; seam-safe values ±1; and the strict distinction between useful complex packaging and physical complex structure.


Certified handoff: 2.XII closes Book 2. It will assemble the dependency graph, normalize historical aliases and retired constructions, certify the final symbol ledger, state exactly which results are new names versus theorems, count axioms/imports/contracts/calibrations, and freeze the transition into Book 3 without importing phase/cophase, mirror, or chirality claims prematurely.


2.XII — BOOK 2 CERTIFICATION: DEPENDENCY, RETIREMENT, AND CLOSURE LEDGER


2.XII.A — Closure criterion


Book 2 is complete when every named operator introduced in the book satisfies five requirements:


• its definition and natural domain are explicit;


• every nontrivial identity used downstream has either a written proof or a cited earlier theorem in the revised dependency chain;


• branch, boundary, and extension behavior are stated without silently widening domains;


• algebraic minimality is distinguished from physical dimensionality;


• and historical terminology, transcription errors, optional representations, and physical interpretations are assigned explicit status rather than allowed to enter by implication.


The present section introduces no new operator construction. Its task is certification.


2.XII.B — Final dependency graph


The revised Book 2 dependency chain is:


Book 1 mathematical constitution

→ 2.I canonical primitive quartet

→ 2.II half-angle atlas and positive phase coordinate

→ 2.III primitive symmetry orbit

→ 2.IV canonically signed UNA differences

→ 2.V first climax: double-angle closure

→ 2.VI second climax: FlatWave binary character

→ 2.VII third climax: Möbius same-phase reduction and one-generator theorem.


Three downstream branches then refine rather than enlarge that algebraic core:


2.VIII extracts the seam-safe harmonic carrier from the reciprocal UNA sum;


2.IX develops the autonomous differential, integral, and asymptotic calculus of the primitive quartet;


2.X assembles the complete boundary atlas from the primitive, UNA, Saw, FlatWave, and carrier layers;


2.XI audits optional complex packaging and retains only the representations justified by exact algebra.


Theorem 2.XII.T1 — No circular dependency in the revised Book 2 chain


No defining theorem in 2.I–2.XI requires a result that is first proved only in a later section.


Proof.


The primitive quartet is defined before any derived operator. The half-angle and symmetry results use only the primitive definitions and Book 1 mathematics. UNA is defined only after the primitive structure is fixed. Double-angle closure uses the already-defined UNA pair. Saw and FlatWave are introduced only after UNA nonvanishing and the signed/unsigned double-angle identities are available. Möbius reduction uses the already-proved FlatWave same-phase equation. The harmonic carrier is extracted from the already-proved reciprocal UNA sum. The calculus and boundary sections use only earlier primitive and derived results. The complex audit packages already-established real quantities. Therefore the logical direction is acyclic. ∎


2.XII.C — Certification of the three planned climaxes


Theorem 2.XII.T2 — Three-climax theorem [D]


The three central reductions of Book 2 are now independently proved and mutually compatible:


FIRST CLIMAX — DOUBLE ANGLE


urx+uxp=4/sin 2x,


urx·uxp=4/|sin 2x|.


SECOND CLIMAX — FLATWAVE


FlatWave

=saw_r+saw_x

=(srx+cxp)/(srx·cxp−1)

=sgn(sin 2x)

∈{−1,+1}.


THIRD CLIMAX — MÖBIUS REDUCTION


cxp

=(srx+ε)/(ε·srx−1),


where


ε=FlatWave=sgn(srx−1).


Consequently the complete named primitive, UNA, Saw, and FlatWave system requires exactly one nonconstant continuous scalar generator on each open quadrant.


Proof.


The first climax is Theorems 2.V.T1 and 2.V.T3. The second is Theorem 2.VI.T2 together with Corollary 2.VI.C1. The third is Theorems 2.VII.T1, 2.VII.T6, and 2.VII.T10. ∎


Corollary 2.XII.C1 — FlatWave is structurally important but not independent data


FlatWave supplies the binary branch appearing in the Möbius formula, but once z=srx is known its value is already determined by


FlatWave=sgn(z−1).


Thus its importance lies in exposing the branch architecture, not in increasing the generator count.


2.XII.D — Final named-operator inventory


The Book 2 objects separate into the following status classes.


Class 1 — Canonical primitive names.


srx=|csc x|+cot x,


sxp=|csc x|−cot x=1/srx,


cxp=|sec x|+tan x,


crx=|sec x|−tan x=1/cxp.


These are definitions applied to exact standard trigonometric expressions. Their names are Projection-specific; their identities are not added axioms.


Class 2 — Canonical derived R operators.


urx=srx−crx,


uxp=cxp−sxp,


saw_r=1/urx,


saw_x=1/uxp,


FlatWave=saw_r+saw_x.


These are deterministic functions of the primitive quartet on D.


Class 3 — Seam-safe harmonic derivatives and representations.


H_D=1/(urx+uxp) on D,


H=sin 2x/4 on R,


V=cos 2x/2 on R,


U=4H,


W=2V,


the carrier map F=(H,V),


and the carrier ellipse C_car.


H is the unique continuous and smooth extension of H_D. These objects regularize the derived double-angle representation; they do not retroactively regularize singular raw operators.


Class 4 — Coordinate and symmetry machinery.


z=srx,


w=cxp,


ε=sgn(sin 2x)=FlatWave on D,


M_ε(z)=(z+ε)/(εz−1),


V₄ for the induced primitive permutation group,


and χ_F for the nontrivial sign character carried by FlatWave.


Class 5 — Optional complex representations.


Ψ_U=urx+i·uxp is retained as an optional diagnostic package of the seam-singular UNA pair.


Z_car=2V+i4H=e^{2ix} is retained as the canonical optional complex form of the smooth carrier.


Neither complex representation is a new primitive or physical state.


2.XII.E — Final domain certification


Theorem 2.XII.T3 — Domain ledger


The natural domains and extension statuses are:


srx,sxp: D_s, the real line with Σ_s removed;


cxp,crx: D_c, the real line with Σ_c removed;


the simultaneous primitive quartet and mixed primitive identities: D=D_s∩D_c;


urx,uxp,saw_r,saw_x,FlatWave,Ψ_U: D;


H_D: D;


H,V,U,W,F,Z_car: all of R;


M_ε: the branch interval I_+=(1,+∞) when ε=+1 and I_-=(0,1) when ε=−1;


one-sided boundary statements: [B];


componentwise split-boundary extensions: [S] only where explicitly proved.


Proof.


Each domain is exactly the one declared and proved in Sections 2.I–2.XI. ∎


Corollary 2.XII.C2 — No silent seam regularization


A finite value of H, V, F, or Z_car at a seam does not define a finite value for an own-channel primitive, a divergent UNA component, a directional Saw trace, FlatWave, or Ψ_U there.


Every object retains its own domain and extension theorem.


2.XII.F — Final information and minimality ledger


Theorem 2.XII.T4 — Static information hierarchy


The following statements are simultaneously true and solve different representation tasks:


1. On every open quadrant, exactly one nonconstant scalar generator is necessary and sufficient for the complete named operator system through FlatWave.


2. On D_s modulo π, z=srx is a bijective positive coordinate, with


θ_s=2 arctan(1/z).


3. The smooth carrier F=(H,V), equivalently Z_car=e^{2ix}, represents phase exactly modulo π on all of R, including the seams.


4. None of these π-periodic representations distinguishes x from x+π.


5. To reconstruct the signed unit-circle point modulo 2π, one additional discrete half-turn label such as α=sgn(sin x) is sufficient.


Proof.


Items 1–5 are respectively Theorems 2.VII.T10, 2.II.T4 with Corollary 2.II.C2, Theorem 2.VIII.T8 and Theorem 2.XI.T10, primitive half-turn invariance from 2.III, and Corollary 2.II.C6. ∎


Corollary 2.XII.C3 — One-generator closure is task-relative

BOOK 2 — CLOSURE SUMMARY AND CONTENTS

Purpose

Book-specific domains, primitive and derived operators, branch signs, coordinate maps, symmetry notation, harmonic carrier notation, and optional complex representations. Canonical absolute-valued primitive definitions and the uxp sign lock are mandatory.

Derive principal and maximal-domain half-angle forms, quadrant transport, exact ranges and midpoint values, inverse phase coordinate modulo π, stereographic recovery, and rational formulas for sin 2x and cos 2x from z=srx.

Prove reflection, quarter-turn, half-turn, and complementary-reflection actions; identify the induced primitive permutation group V₄; distinguish transformed-argument orbit generation from same-phase algebraic reduction.

Define urx=srx−crx and canonical uxp=cxp−sxp. Prove common sign ε, nonvanishing, rational forms, symmetry action, and permanently retire the historical reversed uxp transcription.

Prove

urx+uxp=4/sin 2x,

urx·uxp=4/|sin 2x|,

and the signed/unsigned relation

|urx+uxp|=urx·uxp.

its V₄ sign character, split-boundary behavior, Saw partition identities, and the same-phase cross-family equation.

Solve the same-phase constraint as

cxp=M_ε(srx)=(srx+ε)/(ε·srx−1).

Prove M_ε is a branch-preserving involution, identify it as the z-coordinate form of complementary reflection, derive its fixed point √2+ε, reconstruct the complete named system from z=srx, and prove exact one-nonconstant-generator minimality on each open quadrant.

Define H_D=1/(urx+uxp), extend uniquely to

Prove the four autonomous Riccati equations, arctangent linearization, exact logarithmic antiderivatives, first-order zero–pole seam asymptotics, regular opposite-channel crossings, and differential preservation of one-generator closure.

Assemble primitive, UNA, Saw, FlatWave, |H|, H, and V behavior at sine-zero and cosine-zero seams. Distinguish singular raw operators, bounded split-boundary traces, cusp regularity, and smooth carrier gluing. No physical seam law is inferred.
