Therefore scalar metric modulation can produce nonzero Riemann curvature even though a connection constructed solely as A(φ)dφ cannot. ∎

Interpretive boundary. The independent three-dimensional carrier is doing indispensable work. The scalar changes coefficients on that carrier; it does not replace its independent directions.

4.XI.F — CURVATURE IS NOT TOPOLOGY ALONE

THEOREM 4.XI.P6 — THE SAME LOCAL CARRIER CAN SUPPORT DISTINCT CURVATURE STATES

On the same coordinate domain one may declare

h_F=dx²+dy²+dz²

or

h_H=(L²/z²)(dx²+dy²+dz²).

The first has zero Riemann curvature; the second has scalar curvature −6/L².

Therefore carrier topology and differentiable rank do not uniquely determine the metric curvature. Curvature requires additional geometric data. ∎

COROLLARY 4.XI.P6.1 — THE FLOWER LATTICE DOES NOT SELECT A UNIQUE SMOOTH CURVATURE

The incidence pattern of the tetrahedral center lattice by itself does not determine a continuum metric deformation or Levi–Civita curvature field. Any such assignment requires a metric/gluing prescription beyond adjacency. ∎

4.XI.G — DISCRETE CURVATURE REQUIRES METRIC GLUING DATA

A discrete center graph is combinatorial. To define an angle defect one must add enough metric information to assign lengths and dihedral angles to cells and specify how cells are glued.

Definition 4.XI.G1 — Piecewise-flat edge deficit in three dimensions.

After choosing a simplicial refinement with edge lengths satisfying the simplex inequalities, let Θ_e^σ be the interior dihedral angle at an interior edge e contributed by tetrahedron σ. Define

ε_e=2π−Σ_{σ⊃e}Θ_e^σ.

This is the standard piecewise-flat deficit angle about the edge hinge.

THEOREM 4.XI.P7 — GRAPH ADJACENCY ALONE DOES NOT DETERMINE ε_e

The neighbor graph records which vertices are connected. It does not, by itself, determine all edge lengths, simplex shapes, dihedral angles, octahedral refinement choices, or gluing maps.

Therefore the same abstract adjacency graph can support different metric deficit data.

A nonzero ε_e can be called discrete intrinsic curvature only after the metric simplices and their gluing have been declared. ∎

COROLLARY 4.XI.P7.1 — THE UNDEFORMED FLOWER HONEYCOMB IS NOT CURVED MERELY BECAUSE IT HAS TETRAHEDRA AND OCTAHEDRA

Regular tetrahedral and octahedral cells fitted in the standard Euclidean tetrahedral–octahedral honeycomb form an ordinary flat Euclidean filling. The presence of non-cubical cells does not itself imply curvature.

Any discrete-curvature claim must identify a closure defect, non-Euclidean cell metric, altered gluing, or other invariant geometric obstruction. ∎

4.XI.H — SMOOTH CURVATURE VERSUS DISCRETE DEFECT

THEOREM 4.XI.P8 — SMOOTH AND PIECEWISE-FLAT CURVATURE ARE RELATED BUT NOT IDENTICAL DATA TYPES

Smooth Riemannian curvature is represented pointwise by the Riemann tensor or curvature two-form.

In a piecewise-flat simplicial geometry, each simplex interior is flat and curvature is concentrated on codimension-two hinges through deficit angles.

A continuum limit may relate suitable discrete deficit measures to smooth curvature, but that relation requires a refinement sequence, metric convergence, and scaling control.

Therefore a lattice angle defect must not be silently substituted for a smooth curvature tensor without a declared discrete-to-continuum contract. ∎

4.XI.I — CURVATURE CERTIFICATE

Any later Book 4 or downstream claim of nonzero curvature must provide one of the following equivalent forms of evidence on its declared domain:

1. a connection ω and an explicit nonzero component of

R=dω+ω∧ω;

2. a metric h and an explicit nonzero Riemann/Ricci/sectional/scalar curvature invariant sufficient to establish nonflatness;

3. a metric piecewise-flat complex with explicit nonzero hinge deficit data; or

4. a rigorously stated equivalent holonomy criterion with the necessary local/global qualifications.

Merely displaying variable coefficients does not satisfy the certificate.

4.XI.J — GRAVITY FIREWALL

NEGATIVE THEOREM 4.XI.N2 — CURVATURE DOES NOT SELECT A PHYSICAL SOURCE LAW

Neither

Rᵃ_b≠0

nor

R_scalar≠0

identifies a gravitational field equation, mass source, energy source, coupling constant, equivalence principle, geodesic law for matter, or physical spacetime.

Those require independent physical structure.

Therefore the inference

curvature ⇒ gravity

is prohibited in Book 4. ∎

4.XI.K — SECTION CLOSURE

Book 4 has now established all of the following without a time coordinate:

• a flat reference Flower metric;

• a general positive-definite spatial metric;

• Levi–Civita connection after the metric is chosen;

• exact criteria distinguishing frame variation from curvature;

• an explicit genuinely curved rank-three Riemannian example;

• the one-generator connection-flatness theorem;

• the distinction between scalar connection modulation and scalar metric modulation;

• the metric data required before a lattice defect can count as discrete curvature;

• and the firewall separating mathematical curvature from gravity.

The geometric hierarchy is therefore

carrier

→ metric

→ connection

→ curvature,

with every arrow requiring the stated additional structure.

The next section supplies the remaining geometric datum needed for handed frames, cross products, and orientation-sensitive constructions: a choice of spatial orientation. It then reapplies the earlier orientation obstruction to prove that the symmetric Euclidean carrier can represent either handedness but cannot canonically prefer one.

4.XII — ORIENTATION, VOLUME FORMS, AND HANDED FRAMES

PURPOSE

Book 3 established abstractly that ordered frames separate into two orientation classes and that a mirror frame means negative orientation relative to a declared positive reference orientation. It also proved that mirror reversal is not the same operation as cophase translation, scalar sign reversal, the π half-turn, or the Spin(3) deck map q↦−q.

The present section realizes that abstract discipline on the rank-three Euclidean carrier admitted in Book 4. It defines the spatial volume form, cross product, Hodge star, pseudoscalar sign behavior, and oriented-cell conventions, and then proves directly that the symmetric Euclidean metric and the retained supplementary tetrahedral/lattice witness admit orientation reversal as an exact automorphism. Handedness is therefore representable but not canonically preferred.

Source provenance. The orientation terminology and nonselection logic are inherited from revised Book 0 and Book 3. The historical T3 carrier audit independently reached the same geometric conclusion: O(G) contains both orientation-preserving and orientation-reversing isometries, while cross products and oriented cochains require a chosen volume sign. No physical chirality is imported here. fileciteturn25file7

4.XII.A — ORIENTATION OF THE FLOWER CARRIER

Let U be a connected regular region of F³ with nondegenerate coframe

θ=(θ¹,θ²,θ³).

Definition 4.XII.A1 — Ordered-frame orientation class.

Two ordered coframes θ and θ' belong to the same orientation class when

θ'=Aθ

with

det A>0.

They belong to opposite orientation classes when det A<0.

THEOREM 4.XII.P1 — EXACTLY TWO ORIENTATION CLASSES

The sign of det A is multiplicative under composition and cannot pass continuously from positive to negative without det A=0. Because all admissible frame changes are invertible, the space of ordered frames separates into exactly two connected determinant-sign classes.

Choosing one class to be called positive labels the other negative. ∎

Definition 4.XII.A2 — Mirror frame.

After a positive spatial orientation has been declared, an ordered frame in the opposite determinant-sign class is a mirror frame.

This is the Book 3 definition realized on F³. “Mirror” is relational: it does not mean merely that some coordinate or scalar carries a minus sign. fileciteturn25file14

4.XII.B — VOLUME FORM

Definition 4.XII.B1 — Coframe volume form.

For a positively oriented coframe define

vol_θ=θ¹∧θ²∧θ³.

For a metric h with coordinate matrix h_ij and a chosen positive coordinate orientation, the normalized Riemannian volume form is

vol_h=√(det h)\,dq¹∧dq²∧dq³.

THEOREM 4.XII.P2 — DETERMINANT TRANSFORMATION LAW

If

θ'=Aθ,

then

θ'^1∧θ'^2∧θ'^3

=(det A)\,θ¹∧θ²∧θ³.

Proof. Expand the wedge product multilinearly. Every term with repeated basis one-forms vanishes. The surviving permutation terms sum with their permutation signs, which is precisely the Leibniz formula for det A. ∎

COROLLARY 4.XII.P2.1 — ORIENTATION REVERSAL FLIPS THE VOLUME FORM

If det A<0, then the transformed ordered coframe lies in the mirror class and its oriented volume form has the opposite sign.

Metric lengths and angles need not change. ∎

THEOREM 4.XII.P3 — TETRAHEDRAL FLOWER VOLUME

Let (a,b,c) be the tetrahedral Flower basis with Gram matrix

G₃=r²[[1,1/2,1/2],[1/2,1,1/2],[1/2,1/2,1]].

Then

det G₃=r⁶/2.

Hence the magnitude of the oriented basis-parallelepiped volume is

|vol(a,b,c)|=√(det G₃)=r³/√2.

The regular tetrahedron with those three edge vectors has oriented volume

V_or=(1/6)vol(a,b,c),

so

|V_or|=r³/(6√2)=√2\,r³/12.

The sign records the chosen ordering/orientation; the magnitude is metric. ∎

4.XII.C — CROSS PRODUCT

Assume a positive-definite metric h and a chosen orientation.

Definition 4.XII.C1 — Cross product.

For vectors u,v, define u×v as the unique vector satisfying

h(u×v,w)=vol_h(u,v,w)

for every vector w.

Equivalently,

(u×v)^♭=⋆(u^♭∧v^♭),

where ⋆ is the Hodge star determined by h and the chosen orientation.

THEOREM 4.XII.P4 — CROSS-PRODUCT CHARACTERIZATION

The vector u×v is orthogonal to both u and v and satisfies

|u×v|²=|u|²|v|²−h(u,v)².

Proof. Setting w=u or w=v in the defining volume identity gives zero because the volume form is alternating, hence orthogonality. The magnitude identity follows in an oriented orthonormal basis by direct determinant evaluation and is therefore basis invariant. ∎

COROLLARY 4.XII.P4.1 — AREA IS ORIENTATION-INDEPENDENT; AREA PSEUDOVECTOR SIGN IS NOT

The scalar area magnitude

|u×v|

is unchanged by reversing the spatial orientation. The vector u×v itself changes sign.

Thus orientation-sensitive pseudovectors may faithfully record handedness without selecting which handedness is preferred. ∎

THEOREM 4.XII.P5 — CROSS PRODUCT REVERSES UNDER MIRROR ORIENTATION

Hold h,u,v fixed and replace vol_h by −vol_h. Let ×' denote the resulting cross product. Then

u×'v=−(u×v).

Proof. For every w,

h(u×'v,w)=−vol_h(u,v,w)=h(−u×v,w).

Nondegeneracy of h gives the result. ∎

4.XII.D — HODGE STAR AND ORIENTATION

Definition 4.XII.D1 — Hodge star.

For k-forms α,β on oriented Riemannian F³, define ⋆ by

α∧⋆β=⟨α,β⟩_h\,vol_h.

THEOREM 4.XII.P6 — HODGE STAR CHANGES SIGN UNDER ORIENTATION REVERSAL

If h is fixed and vol_h is replaced by −vol_h, then the corresponding Hodge star satisfies

⋆'=-⋆

on every form degree in three dimensions.

Proof. The inner product of forms is unchanged while the right-hand side of the defining equation changes sign. Uniqueness of the Hodge dual gives ⋆'=-⋆. ∎

COROLLARY 4.XII.P6.1 — CURL IS AN AXIAL/ORIENTATION-SENSITIVE CONSTRUCTION

Because

(curl v)^♭=⋆d(v^♭),

reversing the chosen orientation while holding the metric and vector field fixed reverses the represented curl vector.

This does not mean the underlying exterior derivative d(v^♭) has changed. The sign enters through the orientation-dependent Hodge identification of a two-form with a one-form/vector. ∎

4.XII.E — SCALAR TRIPLE PRODUCTS AND PSEUDOSCALARS

Definition 4.XII.E1 — Oriented scalar triple product.

For u,v,w define

[u,v,w]=vol_h(u,v,w)=h(u×v,w).

THEOREM 4.XII.P7 — TRIPLE-PRODUCT SIGN IS AN ORIENTATION RECORD

Under orientation reversal,

[u,v,w]↦−[u,v,w],

while

|[u,v,w]|

is unchanged.

Therefore the sign of an oriented volume is a pseudoscalar orientation record, not an orientation-selection law. ∎

This is the concrete spatial version of Book 0’s result that orientation-sensitive quantities may change sign under reversal without canonically privileging one sign. fileciteturn25file17

4.XII.F — EXPLICIT FLOWER MIRROR AUTOMORPHISM

THEOREM 4.XII.P8 — BASIS TRANSPOSITION IS AN ORIENTATION-REVERSING ISOMETRY OF THE TETRAHEDRAL FLOWER METRIC

Let S exchange b and c while fixing a:

S(a)=a,

S(b)=c,

S(c)=b.

In the ordered tetrahedral basis, S is represented by

[[1,0,0],[0,0,1],[0,1,0]].

Therefore

det S=−1.

But G₃ is invariant under every permutation of a,b,c because all diagonal entries are r² and all off-diagonal entries are r²/2. Hence

S^T G₃ S=G₃.

Thus S preserves every metric length and angle while reversing orientation. ∎

COROLLARY 4.XII.P8.1 — THE FLOWER METRIC DOES NOT PREFER A HANDEDNESS

The same metric structure admits an exact automorphism exchanging the two frame-orientation classes. Any rule claimed to select “positive” rather than “mirror” while depending only on the symmetric metric data would have to be invariant under S, yet S exchanges the two labels.

Therefore no such canonical selector exists. ∎

THEOREM 4.XII.P9 — THE CENTER LATTICE ALSO ADMITS ORIENTATION REVERSAL

The lattice

Λ₃=ℤa+ℤb+ℤc

is preserved by the basis transposition S because S merely exchanges two integer generators. Hence S is simultaneously

1. a metric isometry,

2. a lattice automorphism, and

3. an orientation reversal.

Therefore adding the undeformed tetrahedral/FCC center lattice does not remove the handedness symmetry. ∎

4.XII.G — NATURALITY / AUTOMORPHISM OBSTRUCTION ON F³

THEOREM 4.XII.P10 — NO CANONICAL ORIENTATION FROM ORIENTATION-SYMMETRIC FLOWER DATA

Let D denote any collection of Flower data invariant under the orientation-reversing automorphism S of Theorems 4.XII.P8–P9: for example the metric, unlabeled lattice incidence, edge lengths, cell congruence classes, or any scalar built naturally from them.

Suppose a canonical rule C(D) selected one of the two orientation classes.

Naturality would require

C(S·D)=S·C(D).

But S·D=D because D is invariant, while S reverses the selected orientation. Hence

C(D)=S·C(D)

would require one orientation class to equal its opposite, which is impossible.

Therefore no canonical orientation can be selected from the orientation-symmetric Flower data alone. ∎

This is Book 0’s automorphism obstruction instantiated on the actual rank-three carrier. The representation problem is solved; the preference problem remains obstructed. fileciteturn25file17

COROLLARY 4.XII.P10.1 — NO ORIENTATION AXIOM IS REQUIRED FOR MATHEMATICS

Whenever an oriented calculation is needed, one may simply declare one of the two orientation classes positive. All orientation-sensitive quantities then acquire consistent signs.

This is an ordinary geometric convention, not a new R-specific law and not a claim that nature prefers the chosen orientation. ∎

4.XII.H — ORIENTED DISCRETE CELLS

Definition 4.XII.H1 — Oriented k-cell.

An oriented edge, face, or tetrahedral cell is a cell supplied with an ordering modulo even permutations. Reversing the orientation multiplies an oriented discrete k-form value by −1.

THEOREM 4.XII.P11 — BOUNDARY OF BOUNDARY VANISHES INDEPENDENTLY OF GLOBAL ORIENTATION LABEL

For the signed incidence operators D₀,D₁,D₂ of an oriented simplicial refinement,

D₁D₀=0,

D₂D₁=0.

A consistent reorientation of cells changes incidence signs coherently but preserves these identities.

Therefore discrete exterior-calculus closure uses orientation while remaining indifferent to which globally consistent orientation is labeled positive. ∎

Interpretation. Oriented combinatorics requires signs; it does not produce a preferred global sign.

4.XII.I — MIRROR, SPIN SIGN, AND PHYSICAL CHIRALITY REMAIN DISTINCT

NEGATIVE THEOREM 4.XII.N1 — SPATIAL MIRROR REVERSAL IS NOT THE SPIN-COVER DECK MAP

The spatial mirror operation belongs to the det<0 component of the spatial orthogonal frame group O(3). By contrast, q and −q in Spin(3) project to the same element of SO(3), which is orientation preserving.

Therefore

mirror frame ≠ q↦−q.

The existence of the spatial Flower orientation does not change Book 3’s distinction. fileciteturn25file0

NEGATIVE THEOREM 4.XII.N2 — SPATIAL HANDEDNESS IS NOT YET PHYSICAL CHIRALITY

A chosen spatial orientation permits the words right-handed and left-handed for ordered spatial frames and orientation-sensitive tensor conventions.

Physical chirality of a fermion or parity-violating interaction requires additional representation and physical law. In particular, nothing in Book 4 chooses a left-chiral projector over its right-chiral mirror.

Therefore

geometric orientation ⇒ capacity to represent handedness,

but

geometric orientation ⇏ preferred physical chirality. ∎

This agrees with the historical T4 Chirality-Orientation Obstruction: reciprocal Projection magnitudes can survive a mirror exchange while the physical choice of left versus right remains external. fileciteturn25file2

4.XII.K — FOURTH CLIMAX: HANDEDNESS EXISTS; PREFERENCE DOES NOT

The rank-three Flower construction resolves the representation question completely. Once a spatial orientation is declared, Book 4 can define positive and mirror frames, oriented volumes, cross products, Hodge duals, curls, pseudoscalars, and oriented discrete cells.

But the same completed Flower geometry possesses an exact orientation-reversing isometry that preserves its metric and lattice. Consequently the neutral carrier has no invariant fact corresponding to “this handedness is the preferred one.”

The final Book 4 result is therefore

boxed statement:

HANDEDNESS IS GEOMETRICALLY REPRESENTABLE.

PREFERRED HANDEDNESS IS NOT SELECTED BY THE ORIENTATION-SYMMETRIC FLOWER CARRIER.

A physical chirality preference, if one is later introduced, must enter through genuinely orientation-sensitive physical structure rather than through relabeling of the existing symmetric geometry.

The final section now audits the entire Book 4 dependency chain and closes the geometric extension before Book 5 is allowed to introduce any further discrete, kinematic, or physical structure.

4.XIII — DEPENDENCY AUDIT, FAILURE LEDGER, AND BOOK 4 CERTIFICATION

PURPOSE

Book 4 began with one obstruction and one discipline.

The obstruction was that the closed Projection scalar calculus of Books 0–3 has one continuous generator and therefore cannot manufacture a rank-two or rank-three spatial coframe from scalar readouts alone.

The discipline was that every new geometric structure required to resolve that obstruction must be introduced visibly, proved where possible, and prevented from acquiring physical meaning merely through familiar terminology.

The book can now close because the resulting dependency chain is explicit from pre-metric Flower construction through rank, metric, lattice, connection, curvature, and orientation.

This final section certifies what has actually been obtained, records every remaining declaration, and retires the historical shortcuts that would otherwise re-enter downstream work.

No arrow in this chain is to be reversed without a separate theorem.

The Projection scalar x and every scalar operator A(x) generated from it satisfy

dA=A'(x)dx

on regular charts. Therefore their one-form span has rank at most one.

The planar rank-two structure enters only when the independent geometric construction supplies two directions. Rank three enters only with the separately admitted noncoplanar extension.

θ¹∧θ²≠0

on F² and

on F³.

Therefore no certified spatial dimension in Book 4 has been extracted from scalar multiplicity, sign sheets, projective lifts, cophase labels, reciprocal operators, or finite branch data. ∎

4.XIII.Q — FINAL BOOK 4 CLOSURE

Book 4 began by asking whether the Projection scalar calculus could somehow be rearranged into the independent directions required for geometry.

The answer is no.

It then asked what minimum independent geometry must be added so that the later theory has somewhere mathematically legitimate to live.

The answer is now explicit.

The Flower construction is retained only as a fixed-radius constructive witness: from copied congruence and local incidence it produces a planar right angle and thereby licenses an ordinary Cartesian metric representation on the declared continuous plane. The subsequent rank-three geometric carrier is an independently admitted noncoplanar extension; it is not obtained by treating Flower coordinates or a tetrahedral tiling as a preferred physical substrate. Standard metric, coframe, connection, curvature, and orientation mathematics may then be developed on that carrier. Any lattice interpretation is downstream and separately declared.

The decisive methodological result is as important as any individual formula:

A missing independent structure must be added as independent structure. It cannot be manufactured by renaming several functions of one variable.

With that rule enforced, the geometric corpus is coherent enough to support the next stage without concealing its assumptions.

BOOK 4 — CERTIFIED CLOSED.

BOOK 5 — AXIOM ZERO AND THE DECADIC CARRIER

5.1 THE INHERITED REAL-FORM SEAM

Let E denote the retained M3 real rank-2 algebraic/orientation carrier, and let V denote the independent M4 real rank-3 geometric carrier. No preferred identification E→V or V→E is inherited from the frozen M0–M4 spine. Define

U = E ⊕ V,

dim_R U = 2 + 3 = 5.

This five is a bookkeeping rank of the neutral real-form carrier. It is not a claim that physical spacetime has five dimensions.

5.2 AXIOM ZERO — REAL–IMAGINARY INDEPENDENCE AND PRIMITIVE NONCOUPLING

Introduce an independent isomorphic partner carrier U^sharp together with a declared block-compatible linear isomorphism ι:U→U^sharp. Write E^sharp:=ι(E) and V^sharp:=ι(V). Require

A0.1  U ∩ U^sharp = {0}, W = U ⊕ U^sharp, and the pairing ι is part of the Axiom-Zero datum. It preserves the earned block ancestry: ι(E)=E^sharp and ι(V)=V^sharp.

A0.2  Primitive admissible symmetry or dynamical operators preserve the two summands separately and lift through the declared pairing as A⊕ιAι⁻¹. Mathematical off-diagonal maps in Hom(U,U^sharp) and Hom(U^sharp,U) may exist, but they are not active primitive couplings. Any later U↔U^sharp coupling must be declared as additional structure.

The notation U^sharp is deliberately used at the structural level so that the independent partner is not confused with the internal M3 operator J. The isomorphism ι is pairing data, not a collapse of U and U^sharp into one real subspace. The internal J and the external complex structure introduced by the paired carrier are distinct objects.

5.3 DECADIC CARRIER THEOREM

Since U^sharp ≅ U and the sum is direct,

dim_R W = dim_R U + dim_R U^sharp = 5 + 5 = 10.

Therefore the number ten is derived from Axiom Zero; ten is not itself the axiom.

With the declared pairing ι, define I_ι(u,v)=(-ι⁻¹v,ιu) on U⊕U^sharp. Then I_ι²=-1, so W has complex rank five. The minimal doubled completion is therefore real rank ten and complex rank five. This complex structure is canonical relative to the declared Axiom-Zero pairing ι; it is not determined by the bare statement U^sharp≅U alone.

Corollary 5.3.1 — Minimality, not ambient uniqueness.  Axiom Zero forces the direct doubled completion W_0 = U ⊕ U^sharp, so dim_R W_0 = 10. It does not assert that no larger carrier can contain W_0. Let an extended carrier take the direct form W_ext = W_0 ⊕ Z, where Z is an additional independent sector. Then dim_R W_ext ≥ 10, with equality exactly when Z = {0}. If the paired-carrier complex structure is required to extend to all of Z, then Z is even-dimensional over R, so

dim_R W_ext = 10 + 2m,   m = 0,1,2,... .

Thus ten is the smallest completed carrier rank forced by the present construction, not a proof that every admissible future realization must have exactly ten real dimensions. Any added sector is new structure and must re-enter through the extension ledger.

5.4 RIVAL OVERLAP COMPLETIONS AND SUPPORTING CONSISTENCY CHECKS

Theorem 5.4.1 — Even-overlap alternatives.  Let I denote the paired-carrier complex structure and suppose, counterfactually, that the real five-space U is allowed to overlap IU. Put k = dim_R(U ∩ IU). Because U ∩ IU is I-invariant, its real dimension is even. Since U has odd real dimension five, U cannot itself be I-invariant. Hence

k ∈ {0,2,4}.

The completed real carrier U + IU then has

dim_R(U + IU) = 10 − k,

so the three admissible ranks are 10, 8, and 6, with corresponding complex ranks 5, 4, and 3. The k=0 case is exactly Axiom Zero. The k=2 and k=4 cases remain lawful mathematical completions if direct independence is weakened. They are not contradictions or failed mathematics; they are rival structures excluded only by the adopted independence axiom.

Supporting consistency checks — nonfoundational.  Several dimension identities independently single out the already-earned five-space U. They support the architecture but do not derive Axiom Zero and are not premises of Theorem 5.3.

First, equality between the dimension of a doubled n-space and its bivector space requires

2n = n(n−1)/2.

For positive integer n, the nonzero solution is uniquely n=5, and both sides then have dimension ten.

Second, the exterior-algebra dimensions of a five-space are

(dim Λ^0U, dim Λ^1U, dim Λ^2U, dim Λ^3U, dim Λ^4U, dim Λ^5U)

= (1,5,10,10,5,1).

Third, if one asks for the Hodge dual of a quadratic bivector product to return in one step to the vector degree,

*(Λ^2U ∧ Λ^2U) ⊂ Λ^(n−4)U,

then the requirement n−4=1 again gives n=5.

These are exact dimension checks. They explain why five and ten recur naturally once U has been earned, but they do not show that all rival carrier constructions have been exhausted. The manuscript therefore treats them as structural corroboration rather than as a necessity proof.

5.5 STATUS AND EXCLUSIONS

M5 is mathematical carrier structure, not a statement of physical spacetime dimensionality. It is the first R-specific mathematical axiom in the revised dependency chain and imports no Standard Model, Spin(10), gauge theory, quantum probability, dynamics, or empirical calibration.

The previously studied 6D and 8D overlap completions remain lawful mathematical counterfactuals. They are excluded from M5 because they violate the adopted direct-independence condition U ∩ U^sharp = {0}; they are not declared impossible mathematics.

Likewise, foundational cross-bivector or SO(5)-type mixing is not inherited at M5. Such mixing can be studied later only as explicitly added structure.

5.6 ANTI-CIRCULARITY FIREWALL

Standard Model content, Spin(10), SU(5), E6/E8, generation counting, CKM structure, string dimensionality, and cosmological coincidences may later test or contextualize the decadic carrier. None may be used retroactively to justify Axiom Zero.

The regular-simplex 10→11 common-mode construction, C^3⊕C^2→C^5 comparison, 16+16 half-spin structure, and bivector/Hodge coincidences are downstream corollaries or comparisons only.

BOOK 6 — LOCAL SYMMETRY AND CARRIER MATHEMATICS

Book 6 exhausts the mathematically available pointwise/frame symmetries, carrier automorphisms, conditional extensions, and obstruction theorems surrounding the certified M5 carrier before physical gauge fields or dynamics are introduced.

6.0 STATUS RULE AND INHERITED BOUNDARY

M0–M4 remain frozen. M3 supplies E, a real oriented Euclidean rank-2 carrier with reflection covariance. M4 supplies V, an independent Euclidean rank-3 carrier with no inherited preferred identification with E. Book 5 supplies U=E⊕V and W=U⊕U^sharp under primitive diagonal noncoupling.

A crucial distinction must be preserved: the absence of primitive E↔V maps is inherited from the independent M3/M4 seam; Axiom Zero separately forbids primitive U↔U^sharp mixing.

Book 6 statements are classified as: (A) direct consequences of the adopted M5 structure; (B) maximal mathematical automorphism envelopes of already-earned structures; (C) conditional extensions requiring an explicitly declared additional mathematical datum; or (D) obstruction/nonselection theorems.

Here “local” means pointwise or frame symmetry only. It does not yet mean a gauge symmetry localized over spacetime.

6.1 INHERITED BLOCK SYMMETRY

For the Euclidean split U=E⊕V, the inherited block-preserving isometry group is

Aut_blk(U,g;E,V) = O(E) × O(V) ≅ O(2) × O(3).

Its identity-component Lie algebra is

so(2) ⊕ so(3),

of dimension 1+3=4.

This is the symmetry earned by the inherited block structure. No larger mixing group is primitive at this stage.

6.2 BIVECTOR DECOMPOSITION AND THE CROSS-BLOCK EXTENSION

The bivectors of U decompose as

Λ²U = Λ²E ⊕ (E⊗V) ⊕ Λ²V,

with dimensions

1 + 6 + 3 = 10.

The first and last pieces reproduce the inherited block Lie algebra. The six-dimensional E⊗V term is the cross-block extension sector. With the full block algebra present, adjoining one nonzero simple cross generator closes under Lie brackets to so(5), taking the closure dimension from 4 to 10.

This is an extension theorem, not inherited structure: SO(5)-type mixing becomes available once a cross-block datum is added, but Book 6 does not promote that datum to a primitive axiom.

6.3 CENTRAL COMPLEXITY OF THE DOUBLED CARRIER

On W=U⊕U^sharp define, using the Axiom-Zero pairing ι:U→U^sharp, the paired-copy complex structure

I_ι(u,v) = (-ι⁻¹v,ιu).

Then I_ι²=-1. Every primitive paired lift A⊕ιAι⁻¹ commutes with I_ι. Hence the M5 primitive representation is complex-linear relative to the declared pairing ι, and W has complex rank five.

This external I_ι is not the internal M3 operator J. Conflating the two would erase the independence introduced by Axiom Zero. Nor should the ι-dependent construction be described as canonical from an unpaired abstract isomorphism class alone.

6.3A CONDITIONAL χ_CHI PARITY-ADAPTED ORBIT EXTENSION

Status: exact carrier algebra conditional on an additional isometric copy-identification; physical mirror-sector interpretation and dynamical transfer are not derived in Book 6.


The doubled carrier of Axiom Zero already contains the algebra needed for a compact copy-to-copy orbit. Let

W = U ⊕ U^sharp

and retain the paired-copy complex structure of §6.3. Introduce, as an additional datum rather than a new axiom, an isometric identification

Π : U -> U^sharp.

If a later physical interpretation takes Π to represent parity, the orientation-reversing requirement belongs only to the relevant geometric subspace. Axiom Zero itself supplies neither that parity interpretation nor a physical mirror world.


Definition 6.3A.D1 — CHI generator.

Define the parity-adapted copy-rotation

J_CHI(u,v) = (-Π^(-1)v, Πu),

for u in U and v in U^sharp. Then

J_CHI^2 = -1.

Relative to the chosen identification Π, J_CHI is conjugate to the paired-copy complex structure I of §6.3. It therefore introduces no second primitive complex structure and no new ambient carrier dimension.


Theorem 6.3A.T1 — CHI orbit.

For a dimensionless parameter χ_CHI,

exp(χ_CHI J_CHI) = cos(χ_CHI) 1 + sin(χ_CHI) J_CHI.

Applied to a state initially in U,

Ψ_CHI(u,χ_CHI)

= exp(χ_CHI J_CHI)(u,0)

= cos(χ_CHI) u ⊕ sin(χ_CHI) Πu.

If Π is isometric, then

||Ψ_CHI||^2 = ||u||^2.

Thus χ_CHI redistributes a fixed carrier norm between the two independent copies without creating additional total amplitude.


Corollary 6.3A.C1 — Complementary copy weights.

For normalized u define

w_U = cos^2(χ_CHI),

w_Usharp = sin^2(χ_CHI).

Then

w_U + w_Usharp = 1,

w_U - w_Usharp = cos(2χ_CHI),

2 sqrt(w_U w_Usharp) = |sin(2χ_CHI)|.

On an oriented chart the sign of the cross-copy coherence is carried by sin(2χ_CHI). The points χ_CHI=0, π/4, and π/2 respectively represent one-copy occupancy, equal copy weight, and complete transfer to the partner copy within this mathematical orbit.


Corollary 6.3A.C2 — Axiom-Zero preservation.

The CHI orbit does not modify

U ∩ U^sharp = {0},

W = U ⊕ U^sharp,

or

dim_R W = 10.

It selects a family of states inside the already-earned decadic carrier. A lower-dimensional orbit or submanifold of states is not a reduction of the ambient carrier dimension.


Firewall 6.3A.F1 — Primitive noncoupling remains in force.

J_CHI is an off-diagonal mathematical endomorphism of the doubled carrier. By A0.2, its existence does not make it a primitive active coupling. Book 6 therefore does not assert that χ_CHI evolves, transfers physical energy or matter between copies, or is observable. Any such activation requires a downstream action, Hamiltonian, connection, boundary condition, or other separately introduced dynamical structure.


Firewall 6.3A.F2 — Physical interpretation is downstream.

This section does not establish that U^sharp is a physical mirror universe, that Π is physical parity, that χ_CHI is spacetime, time, a force coordinate, or an electromagnetic phase, or that the common scalar U(1) of §6.3 is itself observable. It establishes only the exact conditional carrier geometry available if a parity-adapted identification is later earned.


Dependency statement.

Axiom Zero supplies two independent isomorphic copies. Section 6.3 supplies their compatible complex rotation. The present extension shows that a parity-adapted CHI orbit can be defined without altering either result. Later books may test whether χ_CHI acquires an independent physical meaning and whether a lawful interaction activates copy-to-copy transfer.


6.4 PRIMITIVE CENTRAL-IMAGINARY NO-GO

A single copy of the M3 real-type module has no independent scalar complex structure in its commutant; multiplicity two is minimal. The same real-type obstruction holds for the standard SO(3) module. The Axiom-Zero doubling therefore supplies exactly the multiplicity needed for a global central complex structure on W.

6.5 HERMITIAN CARRIER THEOREM

Transport the Euclidean metric of U to U^sharp through ι and take the resulting direct-sum metric on W. It is compatible with I_ι and therefore induces a Hermitian form canonical relative to the declared pairing. Primitive orthogonal block transformations act unitarily after that complex pairing is installed.

6.6 MAXIMAL COMPLEX-LINEAR BLOCK ENVELOPE

If one preserves only the Hermitian metric together with the inherited complex 2+3 block decomposition, the maximal complex-linear isometry group is

U(2) × U(3).

The inherited real-form-preserving subgroup remains only

O(2) × O(3).

The enlargement from the real subgroup to the full unitary group is a mathematical automorphism envelope. It is not yet a physical gauge postulate.

6.7 CENTER THEOREM

The center is

Z(U(2)×U(3)) = U(1)_E × U(1)_V.

The diagonal U(1) is the common scalar phase generated by I. The relative phase is block-dependent and exists because the 2+3 decomposition is preserved. At this stage neither U(1) is identified with electromagnetism or hypercharge.

6.8 CONDITIONAL COMPLEX-VOLUME REDUCTION

If one adds the further mathematical datum of a fixed complex volume form and requires it to be preserved, the envelope reduces to

S(U(2)×U(3)).

Its Lie algebra is

su(2) ⊕ su(3) ⊕ u(1).

The traceless block-scalar direction is unique up to normalization and satisfies

2a + 3b = 0.

A convenient normalization is

a = 1/2,   b = -1/3.

This is a conditional carrier theorem only. The notation is not permission to identify these factors with weak isospin, color, hypercharge, or any physical force.

6.9 FIVE-DIMENSIONAL HODGE-RETURN AND EVEN-CLIFFORD CLOSURE

Let U be an oriented Euclidean n-carrier. For α,β∈Λ²U*,

α∧β ∈ Λ⁴U*,

and therefore

*(α∧β) ∈ Λ^(n−4)U*.

If one imposes the additional Hodge-return criterion that the first genuinely new grade generated by composing orientation bivectors return to the coframe/vector type, then

Λ^(n−4)U* = Λ¹U*,

so n−4=1 and uniquely

n=5.

Equivalently, C(n,4)=n has the unique integer solution n=5 for n≥4.

At n=5 there are ten bivectors. The 15 disjoint bivector pairs generate grade-four forms whose Hodge duals span all five carrier directions. The scalar-plus-bivector sector has dimension 1+10=11, and its one-step even exterior/Clifford closure adds exactly the five grade-four elements:

1 + 10 + 5 = 16.

Thus the grade pattern is

0:1,  2:10,  4:5,

with Λ⁴U canonically Hodge-dual to U.

Status: the mathematics is exact, but the Hodge-return/no-surplus criterion is an additional selector premise. Book 6 therefore records this as an independent rank-five structural certificate, not as a derivation of Axiom Zero.

6.10 UNIVERSAL COMPLEXIFICATION AND THE NO-COMPRESSION RESULT

For the five-dimensional real carrier U, the canonical complexification

U_C = U⊗_R C

has complex dimension five and real dimension ten. Every real-linear map f:U→Z into a complex vector space extends uniquely to a complex-linear map

f_C:U_C→Z,

f_C(u+iv)=f(u)+i f(v).

Now let f be real-injective, let S=f(U), let

m = dim_C span_C S,

and let

k = dim_R(S∩iS).

Then

2m = 10−k,

m∈{3,4,5},

dim_C ker f_C = 5−m,

and

k = 2 dim_C ker f_C.

Therefore faithful complexification is equivalent to k=0. But real injectivity alone does not force this. Explicit real-rank-five embeddings exist into C³ and C⁴, giving respectively k=4 and k=2 while preserving the original five real directions.

This proves a nonselection theorem: the M2 real/projective rank firewall does not by itself forbid lower-dimensional complex completions. Requiring faithful scalar extension simply restates the zero-overlap content needed for Axiom Zero.

A stronger conditional theorem is available. If a complex quotient of E_C⊕V_C is required to be faithful on the real skeleton and equivariant under the uncoupled inherited symmetry, then its kernel must be an invariant complex subspace. The two complexified blocks are inequivalent irreducible summands, so the only invariant subspaces are 0, E_C, V_C, and the whole carrier. Every nonzero option removes certified real-form directions. Hence the unique faithful symmetry-equivariant complex completion is the full C⁵ complexification.

Status: this is a conditional naturality theorem. It does not prove that M0–M4 require complex completion in the first place.

6.11 HERMITIAN, TOTALLY REAL, AND LAGRANGIAN CONDITIONS

Let (W,g,I) be a positive-definite Hermitian real carrier and define

ω(x,y)=g(Ix,y).

For a half-dimensional real subspace U,

ω|_U=0  ⇔  U⊥IU  ⇔  IU=U^⊥.

Any of these conditions implies

U∩IU={0}

and

W=U⊕IU.

Thus Lagrangianity is sufficient for an independent completion.

It is not necessary. Total reality is strictly weaker. In standard C⁵, write U_A=graph(A). Then

ω((x,Ax),(z,Az)) = x^T(A−A^T)z,

so U_A is Lagrangian exactly when A is symmetric, whereas U_A∩IU_A={0} exactly when I+A² is invertible. A nonsymmetric nilpotent example can therefore be totally real without being Lagrangian.

Positive-overlap Hermitian examples also exist with k=2 and k=4. Hence Hermitian compatibility alone does not select k=0.

Status: Lagrangianity would be a sufficient additional axiom, not a discharge of Axiom Zero from M0–M4.

6.12 MINIMAL COMMON COMPLEX-CARRIER THEOREM

Assume a common complex vector space carries faithful commuting complex-linear actions of the internal O(2) symmetry and the geometric SO(3) symmetry, with no preferred identification between the internal plane and a spatial plane.

The internal O(2) factor cannot act faithfully in complex dimension one, because GL(1,C) is abelian whereas reflections reverse the orientation generator. It has a faithful complex two-dimensional realization. The smallest faithful complex representation of SO(3) is three-dimensional.

Complex dimension four cannot carry both faithfully and independently: any faithful SO(3) representation there is 3⊕1, whose commutant is abelian and therefore cannot contain a commuting faithful O(2). In complex dimension five, 3⊕2 has a commutant containing M_2(C), and the required O(2) block exists.

Therefore

dim_C W ≥ 5,

with equality achieved by

W ≅ C³_geom ⊕ C²_orient.

Its underlying real dimension is ten.

Status: this is exact minimality inside the stated common-complex-carrier class. The premise that such a common complex-linear completion is required is additional; the theorem is not a pre-Axiom selector.

6.13 COMPLEXIFICATION–BIVECTOR BALANCE THEOREM

For a real n-carrier U,

dim_R(U⊗_R C)=2n,

whereas

dim_R Λ²U = n(n−1)/2.

Equality requires

2n = n(n−1)/2,

which for n>0 gives uniquely

n=5,

and the common dimension is ten.

More strongly, among nontrivial exterior powers 2≤r≤n−2, the equation

C(n,r)=2n

occurs only for

(n,r)=(5,2) and (5,3),

the Hodge-dual middle pair. At n=5 the exterior dimensions are

1, 5, 10, 10, 5, 1,

so the even and odd exterior sectors each have dimension 16.

Nonselection theorem: equality of dimensions does not imply a natural or equivariant identification between U_C and Λ²U. Their SO(5) characters differ, so they are not the same representation. No inherited principle requires these ranks to match. The balance is therefore an exact fingerprint of the already-earned rank five, not a selector for it.

6.14 FLATWAVE COTANGENT/SYMPLECTIC COMPLETION

For a Möbius transformation

u'=(a u+b)/(c u+d),   ad−bc=1,

one has

du'/du=(cu+d)^(−2).

Preservation of the canonical one-form p du gives

p'=p(cu+d)²,

so

p' du'=p du

and

dp'∧du'=dp∧du.

Under the reciprocal coordinate

s=sqrt(1+u²)−u,

with inverse

u=(1−s²)/(2s),

the dual coordinate transforms as

p_s = p_u du/ds = −(1+s²)p_u/(2s²).

For any real n-dimensional Euclidean carrier (U,G),

T*U ≅ U⊕U*

has real dimension 2n. The metric-dependent block operator

J_G(q,p)=(-G^(-1)p,Gq)

satisfies J_G²=-1 and is compatible with the canonical symplectic form. At n=5 this gives a ten-real-dimensional cotangent completion.

The no-go is equally important: the functorial cotangent lift exists for every smooth carrier and does not imply that cotangent covectors are primitive state variables. Pre-Axiom Projection structure does not supply the rule U→T*U as a state completion. Thus this route corroborates the M5 doubling but does not derive it. Identifying U^sharp with U* requires an explicit metric/duality choice, and cotangent variables cannot be called physical momentum before a later action or dynamics is supplied.

6.15 REGULAR-SIMPLEX COMMON-MODE THEOREMS

For arbitrary N≥2, let d=N−1 and define

V_d={ξ∈R^N : Σ_A ξ_A=0}.

The operator

P_rel = I − (1/N)J

is the orthogonal projector onto V_d. The centered vectors

v_A=e_A−(1/N)1

are the vertices of a regular d-simplex and satisfy

|v_A|²=(N−1)/N,

v_A·v_B=−1/N   for A≠B,

and

|v_A−v_B|²=2.

Choosing one vertex as origin and normalizing the d outgoing edges gives the Gram matrix

G_d=(1/2)(I_d+J_d),

with spectrum

{(d+1)/2, 1/2 repeated d−1 times}

and determinant

det G_d=(d+1)/2^d.

This theorem is universal in N. The cases d=3 and d=10 are examples only; the simplex theorem does not select ten.

Under the standard S_N action on V_(N−1), the simplex metric is preserved and

det(T|V_(N−1))=sgn(T).

Therefore A_N preserves orientation and every odd permutation exchanges the two orientation classes. Symmetric simplex data contain both orientations but canonically select neither.

This orientation obstruction also holds for every N≥2 and therefore cannot select N=11 or d=10.

6.16 TERMINAL FIREWALL

No passage from a pointwise carrier automorphism group to a spacetime-dependent gauge group is permitted without additional structure: a base space or bundle, local sections, a connection, curvature, and ultimately a dynamics or action principle.

Accordingly Book 6 stops before physical gauge fields, spacetime localization, action principles, dynamics, quantum probability, particle ontology, physical chirality choice, hypercharge assignment, coupling constants, masses, mixing matrices, anomaly cancellation, generation counting, or physical force names.


VOLUME I CLOSURE — BOOKS 0–6


Volume I closes the internal mathematical reconstruction before classical dynamics. Books 0–6 establish the source boundary, mathematical constitution, canonical R-operator calculus, projective phase and orientation structure, geometric rank and coframe/connection boundary, Axiom Zero, and the pointwise local-symmetry/carrier mathematics.


DEPENDENCY BOUNDARY TO VOLUME II


Volume II may inherit only the mathematical structures and explicit assumptions certified here. Book 6 does not itself supply physical gauge fields, spacetime localization, action principles, dynamics, quantum probability, particle ontology, physical chirality, hypercharge, coupling constants, masses, mixing matrices, anomaly cancellation, generation counting, or force identification. Those structures must enter downstream through their stated extensions or physics imports.
